      33  - **Decided by the builder of the pass, for the lead and Markus
           to confirm: the names of SQLite's eponymous virtual tables are
           "not supported".** They are `json_each`, `json_tree`, `jsonb_e
          ach`, `jsonb_tree`, and `pragma_<name>` for 59 PRAGMAs. SQLite
          reads these tables without a CREATE, and an engine with a catal
          og of its own would answer `no such table`. This goes beyond th
          e roadmap's restrictions.
      34  - **Open, and no slice needs it yet: whether s4ql promises SQLi
          te's order of rows for a query without `ORDER BY`.** Slice 1 re
          turns rows in primary-key order, as SQLite does for its tables.
           With joins and indexes, SQLite's order follows its query plann
          er.
      35  - **Decided on 2026-11-18: the statements of a transaction that
           changes nothing are stored nowhere.** Such a transaction write
          s no commit object, so its annotations are dropped. Examples ar
          e an `insert` that fails on a key that exists and a `drop table
          ` of a table that does not exist. Markus agreed to keep it so:
          annotations explain the results in the log, and without results
           there is nothing to explain or to replay. The spec's wording f
          its, because it speaks of the statements that produced the resu
          lts. If every statement is to be kept one day, for an audit for
           example, that needs a place of its own, since a commit object
          without changes would only make the log grow.
      36 +- **To consider: how a developer learns what is not supported i
         +n a query.** Today a statement that s4ql does not support fails
         + when it runs, with an error that names one thing. Markus asked
         + on 2026-11-19: "how does a developer know what's not supported
         + based on the incoming query?" Questions to answer: whether the
         + error names the exact construct and where it is in the text, w
         +hether it reports all unsupported things in a statement or only
         + the first, whether the error can be told apart in code (a sent
         +inel or a type), and whether there is a way to check queries be
         +fore they run, such as a tool over an application's SQL or a li
         +st of what is supported in the documentation. Not decided.

❯ There'll be some YAGNi vs. principle of least surprise tradeoffs on our       
  journey.                                                                      

⏺ Agreed. Overnight I'll resolve them the way we have so far: reject loudly what
  we have not decided to support, because loosening later breaks nobody and
  tightening does. The surprise is then an error with a clear message, never a
  silently different result from SQLite. Each case I meet goes into the roadmap
  or the todo, so you can overrule it.
✻ Brewed for 3s · done Thursday 9:59 PM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The storage format and the storage core now handle all five types, with exact
  types, -0.0 stored as 0.0, and a new `core.ErrTooBig`. The work is on branch
  `types-core`, based on `origin/binding-types`: 9 commits, not pushed. `make
  test`, `make lint` (including `lint-proto`) and `make check-browser` pass. The
  new tests pass with `-count=20`, with and without `-race`, and so does `go
  test -race -short ./internal/core/`. Nothing in this brief conflicts with the
  spec. I changed nothing in `internal/sqlite`, `internal/engine` or
  `internal/simulator`: the engine compiles unchanged and its tests pass.

  ## Format
  - `enum Type` gets `TYPE_INTEGER=2`, `TYPE_REAL=3`, `TYPE_TEXT=4`,
  `TYPE_BLOB=5`. `int` and `integer` stay apart. The code was regenerated with
  `make generate`.
  - In a row or a primary key, the reader and the writer now refuse text with
  NUL, an infinite real, and -0.0 (new `checkStored` in
  `internal/format/check.go`).
  - In an annotation parameter, the writer refuses NaN, infinities and text with
  NUL, but keeps -0.0 with its sign. The reader still checks no annotations.
  - The sample `commit-v0-all.bin` gained a table `every` with a column of each
  type, edge rows and a -0.0 parameter. A new subtest fails if no sample holds
  some enum value.
  - The README of `internal/format` explains `INT` vs `INTEGER`,
  NUL/NaN/infinity, and the -0.0 rule.

  ## Core
  - **Declared types:** `typeNames`/`typeOf` in `cache.go` declare and load the
  five types, compared without case, because SQLite reports strict types in
  capitals.
  - **Reading and binding:** `bind` handles reals, text and blobs, and binds
  -0.0 as 0.0. `readRow` reads all kinds.
  - **Exact types:** `table.checkValue(position, v, limit)` checks them before
  SQLite sees the value. It refuses NaN and infinities in a real, and invalid
  UTF-8 or NUL in text, with `ErrInvalid`.
  - **Too-long values:** `ErrTooBig` is not wrapped in `ErrInvalid`. A text or
  blob longer than the limit is refused before SQLite. SQLite's `SQLITE_TOOBIG`
  (code 18) from the row write in `Upsert` also becomes `ErrTooBig`, and the
  transaction stays usable.
  - **The limit:** an unexported option `lengthLimit` (default 1,000,000,000,
  set with `SetLengthLimit` on every connection). Tests lower it through
  `Options.SetLengthLimit` in `export_test.go`.
  - **Touched keys:** this changed more than expected. The single temporary
  table with an `existed` column is replaced by two key-only tables,
  `s4ql_touched_N` and `s4ql_existed_N`.
    - The key is recorded after the write succeeds.
    - `Delete` records a key only if it found the row, but still marks the table
  as changed so the order of tables in the commit object stays the same.
    - `spoolTouched` reads with a `cross join`, so SQLite sorts nothing.
  - **Planted bug `blobCut`**, at `Tx.committed`: the commit object cuts a blob
  at its first zero byte.

  ## Decisions of detail
  - **-0.0:** the core stores it as 0.0 and does not refuse it, which is what
  the roadmap's slice 4 says. SQLite would store 0.0 anyway; `bind` normalising
  it is a second guard. The commit object is written from the cache, so it holds
  0.0. Annotations keep -0.0. Tested for the cache, the commit object,
  Get/Delete with -0.0, and replay.
  - **Too-long value:** `ErrTooBig` is returned exactly where SQLite's own table
  refuses the row. SQLite counts the whole record of the row, header included,
  so the boundary is a few bytes below the limit and depends on the table's
  shape.
    - That is why the temporary tables hold keys only: the old `existed` column
  made the key's record one byte longer than the row's, so the core would refuse
  a row that SQLite's own table accepts.
    - It is also why the commit query must not sort: a sorter's record holds the
  key and the row together. `TestTx_tooBig` caught this with a `union all`
  design, which failed the commit with SQLite's "string or blob too big".
    - `TestTx_tooBig` uses a limit of 1000 and compares every length from 980 to
  1002 with SQLite's own table, in four table shapes, both for a table created
  in the transaction and one that existed before.
  - **Cache version stays 1.** A table of `int` columns is declared exactly as
  before, and the layout itself is unchanged; temp tables never reach the file.
  An older version that meets a table of a new type refuses it with
  `ErrCacheVersion` when it first looks it up, and a commit object with a new
  type as `ErrNewerVersion`; it never misreads one. `TestOpen_cache` builds a
  cache with the old statements and opens, reads and changes it.
  - `docs/todo.md`: I removed the open question on `integer` (it is now a second
  value), marked NUL in text as decided by the roadmap but not yet built in the
  pass or engine, and recorded these decisions.

  ## Tests
  - `TestScan_order`: for each type, the scan order equals SQLite's own table
  with the same keys, and the model's order.
  - `TestTx_types`: every type, plus a column of every type beside the key. It
  compares the commit object with the model, compares both caches (the
  committing instance's and the replayed one) bit for bit with `cacheDump`, and
  checks -0.0 and every wrong kind.
  - `TestTx_tooBig`: described above.
  - **Test model:** `compareKeys` orders each kind as SQLite does. `stored()`
  turns -0.0 into 0.0. `text()` writes values exactly (`r-0`, `"..."`, `x'..'`).
  `describe` names types other than int. `cacheDump` reads every type. The edge
  values are in `edges` in `model_test.go`.
  - **`TestTx_Savepoint_random`:** random key and value types, keys from
  `edges`, refused and too-big upserts, and a bitwise comparison of the replayed
  cache. New minimums are pinned, and the old ones still hold.
  - **Simulation:** four new table shapes, including a two-column key of a blob
  and a real. `simKeys` puts 0.0 and -0.0 among the first three reals. New trace
  markers count upserts and deletes of text keys, blob keys and -0.0, with
  minimums pinned at a quarter for the single, full and hammer workloads. The
  old minimums all hold. The traces are still deterministic.
  - **`blobCut` is found:**

  | Where | Seeds that found it | Found anything without the bug |
  |---|---|---|
  | Simulation, default run | each of the first 4 | 0 |
  | Simulation, 1000 seeds | 716 | 0 |
  | `TestTx_Savepoint_random`, planted by hand | 84 of 400 | not run |

    A key listed twice ("is no net effect") is now one of its symptoms, because
  cutting `x'00'` and `x'0000'` makes them one key.

  ## Run times
  These were on a machine with other work running, so the differences are within
  noise. The 53 s run had my code-review running beside it.

  | Run | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | `go test -run '^TestSimulation' ./internal/core/` | 44 s | 40 s, then 53 s |
  | `TestTx_Savepoint_random` | 2.0 s | 2.5 to 3.0 s |
  | `go test -short ./internal/core/` | 56 s | 58 to 60 s |

  ## Self-review
  The code-review skill at medium effort over
  `origin/binding-types...types-core` found no bugs and one nit: the doc
  comments of `Tx.Annotate` and `ReadTx.Get` did not list the new refusals. Both
  are fixed.

  ## What is left
  - The engine must turn `core.ErrTooBig` into SQLite's "string or blob too big"
  (code 18). It must also refuse NUL, infinities and wrong kinds itself,
  because `fromCore` reports `ErrInvalid` as an internal fault.
  - Mutation testing of the new core code is out of scope, as the brief says.

  ## What a reviewer should look at
  - `internal/core/tx.go`: `Tx.Upsert`, `Tx.Delete`, `Tx.touch`,
  `Tx.spoolTouched` (the `cross join`), and `Tx.committed`.
  - `internal/core/cache.go`: `checkValue`, `bind`, `readRow`, `typeOf`.
  - `internal/format/check.go`: where -0.0 is refused, and where it is not.
  - Whether the new simulation shapes and pinned minimums in
  `internal/core/workloads_test.go` are the right ones.

✻ Cooked for 35s · done Thursday 10:09 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Verdict: merge as is.** I found nothing that can corrupt or lose data. `go
  test -short ./internal/core/ ./internal/format/` passes (core 52s, format
  0.2s). I mutated four spots to probe the tests and restored the worktree after
  each; it is clean, and nothing was pushed.

  **1. Net effect per key (`Upsert`, `Delete`, `touch`, `spoolTouched`): correct
  in every sequence I traced.**
  - Upsert, then delete, of a key that did not exist: the key is in `touched`
  only, so nothing is written.
  - Delete, then upsert, of a key that existed: the key is in both tables and
  the row is there, so one upsert.
  - Delete of a key that never existed: `found` is false and nothing is
  recorded.
  - A failed upsert is caught by `checkRow` before SQLite, or by a TOOBIG
  statement rollback, before `touch`.
  - Savepoints: `Savepoint` copies each `txTable`, names included, and SQLite's
  savepoint rolls back the temp DDL and rows with it. A first touch undone by
  `RollbackTo` is recorded again later with the right `existed`. `tx.temps` is
  not rolled back, so names stay unique.
  - An `int` key and a `real` key cannot meet, because each strict column has
  one type and `checkValue` enforces the kind.
  - `0.0` and `-0.0` compare equal in SQLite, and NaN is refused, so no other
  pair of reals can collide.
  - Commit order is deterministic: an explicit `order by` on the temp table's
  primary key.
  - Mutations: passing `existed=false` from `Upsert` is caught by
  `TestTx_Savepoint_random`. `TestTx_types` misses it, because it only deletes
  before upserting again. Recording `existed` on a later touch (dropping the
  `first == 0` guard) is caught by `TestTx_Savepoint_random` and
  `TestTx_tooBig`.

  **2. Values reaching SQLite (`checkValue`, `bind`, `readRow`, `typeOf`): no
  conversion is possible.** Kinds must match the column type exactly, and
  SQLite's integer storage of integral reals in a REAL column reads back bit for
  bit. An empty blob and an empty text never meet, since their columns differ.
  `BindBlob` binds nil as an empty blob, and a nil `Blob` is no value, which
  `checkValue` refuses. The commit object is built from rows read back from the
  cache, so it holds what the cache holds.

  **3. Reader and writer (`format/check.go`): symmetric.** Both use
  `checkRow`/`checkPrimaryKey` → `checkStored` for rows and keys. Annotations
  are stricter only on the write side. The reader does not check annotation
  parameters, so a log written before this change cannot become unreadable. Old
  logs only hold `int` and NULL rows, which the new refusals do not touch.

  **4. `ErrTooBig`: correct.** Code 18 is SQLITE_TOOBIG. `checkValue` refuses a
  length above the limit, the same boundary as SQLite's bind check. A row too
  big for SQLite fails the statement, which SQLite rolls back alone;
  `TestTx_tooBig` shows that the old row of key 1 survives an oversized
  replacing upsert, and that the transaction goes on and commits. Replay applies
  the same limit, so a row the writer took is always taken on replay.

  **5. Keeping the cache version at 1: agree.** Temp tables do not persist. An
  old binary refuses a new cache's non-`int` column with `ErrCacheVersion`, and
  refuses new commit objects through the unknown enum value. Bumping the version
  would not make a downgrade work either.

  **6. Tests:** they are model-based and compare the exact commit-object lines
  plus a bitwise dump of a replayed cache, so they are strong. The diff adds no
  `Skip`, `Short` or `Sleep`. Under `-short`, `TestTx_Savepoint_random` runs 40
  seeds and logs "NOT CHECKED" for the reach counts, which is a reduced run, not
  a skip.

  **Minor notes (nothing to fix):**
  - a. `bind` in `cache.go`: the `-0.0` → `0.0` normalisation is not pinned by
  any test. I removed it and every test still passed, because SQLite already
  stores `-0.0` in a REAL column as integer 0. It is harmless and correct as a
  safeguard, but the doc comment's "so that no … temporary table holds the sign"
  holds even without it.
  - b. `spoolTouched`: the `cross join` is not pinned either. With a plain
  `join`, `TestTx_tooBig` still passes, because SQLite's planner already chooses
  that plan. It is correct either way.
  - c. Only `TestTx_Savepoint_random` covers "upsert of a key that existed, then
  delete it". A direct case in `TestTx_types` would make that path visible
  without the random test. Optional.

✻ Cogitated for 10s · done Thursday 10:14 PM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  ## Summary

  Branch `types-stmt` (worktree
  /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a08351f16b7712cdb), 4
  commits on top of `origin/binding-types`. Nothing is pushed.

  The statement pass now supports the column types and literals of slice 4.
  Every supported literal is checked against SQLite. A real literal gets
  SQLite's value bit for bit because the pass uses an exact port of SQLite's own
  conversion routine. On 100,000 hard-to-round texts the port matched SQLite
  every time, and Go's `strconv.ParseFloat` disagreed with SQLite on 6,998 of
  them. `make test`, `make lint`, `make check-browser` and the crosscheck module
  all pass. I did not touch `internal/sqlite`.

  One difference from your brief: I changed the driver's `check` in `/conn.go`,
  which you had put out of scope (details under "What else changed").

  ## What I built

  - **Column types.** `create table t (v <type> primary key) strict, without
  rowid` works with `int`, `integer`, `real`, `text` or `blob`, in any case of
  the letters.
    - `stmt.CreateTable.Type` is a new `stmt.Type`: `TypeInt`, `TypeInteger`,
  `TypeReal`, `TypeText` or `TypeBlob`. Its `String` gives the name SQLite
  records (`INT`, `INTEGER`, ...), and its zero value means no type.
    - Any other type is not supported, under the new name `column type other
  than INT, INTEGER, REAL, TEXT, or BLOB`. That covers quoted types (SQLite
  dequotes `"text"`), sizes like `text(10)`, two-word types, `any` and unknown
  names like `varchar`.
    - An unknown type stays "not supported" rather than getting SQLite's error.
  SQLite's `unknown datatype for t.v: "foo"` is raised in `sqlite3EndTable`,
  after the parse, so it is not a syntax error. That makes "not supported" the
  answer the pass's own rule gives, the same as for `int(10)`, so it is not a
  known gap.
  - **Literals.** `stmt.Insert.Value` is now a sealed interface, `stmt.Value`,
  with four types: `Integer` (int64), `Real` (float64), `Text` (string) and
  `Blob` ([]byte). It is nil for a parameter. A blob literal never gives a nil
  slice, and `x''` is an empty blob.
    - Reals in every form SQLite's tokenizer accepts, with at most one `-`;
  `-0.0` keeps negative zero.
    - Strings in single quotes, with `''` read as `'`.
    - Blobs as `x'..'` or `X'..'`, hex digits in either case.
  - **The engine, until its builder comes.** It refuses another column type with
  `s4ql: not supported: column type REAL` (or INTEGER, TEXT, BLOB). It refuses
  a non-integer literal with `s4ql: not supported: a real literal, which is not
  an integer` (or string, blob). Both match `stmt.ErrNotSupported`.
    - The refusal comes after the text's own errors and before the argument
  checks. It also applies in `engine.Check`.
    - `NewPlan` panics for such a statement.
    - Tests are in `/internal/engine/engine_test.go` and
  `/internal/engine/plan_test.go`.

  ## How real literals are read, and why it is exact

  `/internal/sql/stmt/real.go` is a line-for-line port of SQLite 3.54.1's
  `sqlite3AtoF`, `sqlite3Fp10Convert2`, `powerOfTen`, `sqlite3Multiply128` and
  `sqlite3Multiply160` (lines 37290 to 37760 of `internal/sqlite/c/sqlite3.c`).
  It uses the same integer arithmetic, with `math/bits` for the products.

  - SQLite stops taking digits after about 19, so for longer literals it can
  round to a float other than the nearest. SQLite's own example,
  `3500000000000000.2500001`, gives `3500000000000000.0`; Go gives `...0.5`. The
  pass gives SQLite's answer.
  - I chose the port because using Go's parser with a bound would have rejected
  literals that SQLite reads fine.
  - The evidence:
    - `TestCheck_reals` compares 100,000 hard-to-round texts with SQLite's
  `select <literal>`: 81,351 reals and 18,649 infinities, every one matching.
    - `FuzzLiteral` ran 7,192,877 executions locally and found nothing.
    - `TestGenerate` produces 20,000 statements, and its reals come from
  `strconv`. So they also confirm that SQLite rounds the shortest digit strings
  to the nearest float.
  - Every line of `real.go` is covered. The reviewer also compared the port with
  SQLite's C line by line and found it the same.
  - If a later SQLite changes these routines, the port must change with it, and
  `TestCheck_reals` would show the difference.

  ## Decisions of detail (all recorded in `docs/todo.md`)

  - `-'a'` and `-x'01'`: SQLite turns both into the integer 0, so the pass
  rejects them as `string after a sign` and `blob after a sign`.
  - A literal SQLite reads as an infinity (`1e999`, `-1e999`) is rejected as
  `infinite real number`.
  - An underflow such as `1e-400` is supported and gives `0.0`, as in SQLite.
  - A bare `true` or `false` is rejected as `TRUE or FALSE`; SQLite reads them
  as 1 and 0.
  - A double-quoted `"a"` stays `column reference`. The pinned SQLite build has
  `DQS=0`, so SQLite treats it as a column too.
  - The names `real number`, `string` and `blob` are gone from the vocabulary.
  - `stmttest` still generates only INT and integers. The pass's tests wrap it
  in `generate_test.go`, replacing the type or the literal in one statement out
  of two.
  - The engine's refusal messages follow the style of its existing argument
  message. They carry no offset and no value.
  - The open todo item on NUL in text values now reads "decided by the roadmap,
  partly built". A string literal can never hold a NUL because the SQL text is
  checked first; refusing a text argument with a NUL is still to build.

  ## Pinned numbers that moved

  All of them are in the pass's tests:

  | Test | Before | After |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `TestGenerate_mutants`, syntax errors where SQLite does not say | 3 | 1 |
  | `TestGenerate_mutants`, not supported where SQLite does not say | 360 | 367
  |
  | `TestGenerate_nearMisses`, excused | 1,046 | 985 |

  - **Mutants.** The generator now replaces the type or literal in half its
  statements, so the mutants of those statements are different texts. Two of the
  three syntax errors were mutants of CREATE TABLE statements whose type is now
  replaced. All 367 are mutants of CREATE TABLE.
  - **Near misses.** SQLite stops `AUTOINCREMENT` after a non-INTEGER type with
  a grammar-action error, which the oracle excuses from comparison. With an
  INTEGER column, SQLite parses the text and refuses AUTOINCREMENT only later,
  for WITHOUT ROWID. So the 61 near misses of that form whose statement now has
  an INTEGER column are compared instead of excused (516 → 455). The other
  excused forms are unchanged (516, 7, 7).
  - **Corpus.** Supported texts went from 727 to 1,198, with 471 more INSERTs;
  this count is not pinned. The excused counts of the corpus, corpus mutants and
  grammar tests did not move.
  - **Engine and driver.** The pinned counts of the engine's differential test
  and the driver's differential test (`drivercheck`) did not move, so I had
  nothing to explain there.

  ## What else changed, and where I went past the brief

  - **The driver's Prepare.** Your brief put the driver out of scope, but the
  review found that `Prepare` would accept a statement the engine then refuses
  only when it runs. Before slice 4, Prepare refused these texts. So the
  driver's `check` in `/conn.go` now also calls `engine.Check(query,
  s.Parameters())` for a supported statement, and `TestPrepare` checks two such
  texts. Because of this, drivercheck treats these texts as refused by their
  text, exactly as before, and its pinned numbers stay those of `main`.
  - **Small compile fixes.** `stmttest` now sets `Type: stmt.TypeInt` and wraps
  the integer value. The doc comment of `stmttest.Others` mentions the engine's
  refusal. drivercheck asks `engine.Check` before applying its argument rule,
  and two of its pinned steps were updated.

  The engine and the driver now handle all five column types end to end on
  branch `types-engine` (HEAD 0bb467c, 7 commits on 58e7b34; nothing pushed).
  `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser` pass. The new tests also
  pass with `-count=20` and under `-race`, and the engine simulation passes at
  400 and 1,000 seeds. 58e7b34 is in `origin/main`, and `origin/main` (4b53387,
  the merge of #69) adds no changes on top of it, so the branch merges cleanly.

  **What I built**
  - **Engine** (`internal/engine`):
    - INT and INTEGER stay apart from the plan through the replay.
    - Real, string and blob literals are evaluated.
    - `checkTypes` is gone.
    - Before the instance, the engine refuses arguments no column holds: NULL, a
  value of no kind, NaN, ±Inf, text with NUL, and text that is not valid UTF-8.
    - The type check is a new step of the sink (`insert.check`).
    - `core.ErrTooBig` becomes SQLite's 18/18 `string or blob too big` in
  `fromCore`.
    - The in-memory test double holds every type and compares keys as SQLite
  does.
  - **Core** (small change): `lengthLimit` is now the exported
  `core.Options.LengthLimit`, which must be from 30 to 1e9, and
  `DB.LengthLimit()` reads it. The tests of the engine and the driver lower it
  to 1000; `SetLengthLimit` in `internal/core/export_test.go` is gone. This is
  how the tests avoid a gigabyte.
  - **Driver**:
    - It takes `float64`/`float32`, `string` and a non-nil `[]byte` (empty means
  the empty blob), and named types through database/sql's conversion.
    - A nil `[]byte` gives `argument 1 of type []byte, which is nil`.
    - Every blob a query returns is a fresh copy, never nil.
    - `check` calls only `stmt.Check`; the second `engine.Check` is gone.
    - The package doc describes the new types.
  - **Tests**:
    - Engine: `TestEngine_types` (edges of each type through literal and
  argument, each twice, against SQLite, plus a replay, INT/INTEGER, -0.0, blob
  copy) and `TestEngine_tooBig` (every length from 988 to 1002 against SQLite at
  limit 1000, the order of errors also in a read-only transaction, the
  transaction stays usable).
    - Engine transactions: three new typed cases in `TestConn_Begin`.
    - Driver: `TestTypes` in `conn_test.go`.
    - Driver check: `TestTypes` in drivercheck runs the edges of every type
  against mattn, pinned and through the pool, with prepared runs, other-type
  values and a replay.

  **Decisions of detail** (recorded in `docs/todo.md` and the engine README, new
  section "Types")
  - **Order of errors for an insert:** errors of the text and arguments → names
  → too big for an argument (SQLite's bind) → readonly in a read-only
  transaction (SQLite's `OP_Transaction`) → "not supported" for the type
  (`OP_TypeCheck`) → UNIQUE → too big for a record whose value fits but whose
  row does not. Wherever SQLite gives an error that s4ql reproduces, s4ql gives
  the same one.
  - **Messages:** `s4ql: not supported: argument 1, which is text, for the
  column t1.v1 of type INT` and `… a string literal for the column t1.v1 of type
  INT`, with names as the table stores them. A type refusal leaves the count of
  changes untouched.
  - **Value refusals:** `argument 1, which is NULL` / `has no value` / `is a
  real that is not a number` / `is an infinite real` / `is text that holds the
  character NUL` / `is text that is not valid UTF-8`.
  - **Too-long literals:** the engine gives too big for them as well, but the
  tests don't compare that with SQLite. With default limits SQLite gives
  `statement too long` first, and with only `LENGTH` lowered it turns a too-long
  blob literal into NULL.
  - **Open gap, noted in todo:** s4ql gives no `statement too long` for a text
  over 1e9 bytes.

  **Pinned numbers that moved.** String, real and blob arguments no longer fail
  before the instance.
  - Engine `TestDifferential`: the 85 "not an integer" calls are now 50 "an
  argument that no column holds" plus 10 "a value of another type than its
  column". The other 25 meet SQLite's name errors and are compared with SQLite.
  - Driver `TestGenerated`: `excusedArgument` 109→85, new `excusedType` 8,
  `excusedPrepare` 1051→1064, `excusedOutside` 43→46, `excusedRowsOpen` 44→45.
  Of the 24 calls with a string, real or blob argument, 8 are type refusals, 7
  are prepare excuses, 1 outside, 1 rows-open, and 7 are compared with mattn; I
  counted these with a temporary tally. The remaining +6 prepare and +2 outside

  "not supported" and now make tables.
  - The test rules changed to match: new `pair.checkType` in the engine's
  differential test, and `argumentRefusal`, `reaches`, `checkRefused` and
  `typeRefusal` in drivercheck. The oracle binds each argument by its type, and
  `oracleValues` replaces `integerValues` in the simulation.

› Message from general-purpose
  I found nothing on `types-engine` that matters: merge it as is.

  `go test -short ./internal/engine/ .` passes. So do `go build ./...`, `go
  vet`, `golangci-lint` on engine, root and core (0 issues), the js/wasm build
  with `s4ql_browser`, and `go test -short -tags s4ql_browser
  ./internal/simulator/...`. I wrote throwaway driver tests in the worktree, ran
  them, and deleted them; the worktree is clean.

  What I checked, by your six points:

  1. **Can a wrong or refused value reach the core?** No path I could find.
     - Arguments: every value passes `checkArgument` in `prepare`, on every
  path.
     - Literals: the statement pass guarantees them (finite reals, valid UTF-8
  text with no NUL, non-nil blobs). `eval` refuses a nil blob as well.
     - Every path that can write runs `buildSink`, then `s.check()`, then `run`:
  `write`, `writeIn` and the planted `checkOutside`. The read-only branch never
  calls `run`.
     - `insert.run` iterates the same `values.data` that `check` saw, and each
  run parses and plans the text again, so a prepared statement run twice is
  checked twice.
     - Confirmed by running: a wrong type inside a write transaction is refused
  and the transaction then commits. A prepared statement refuses a wrong type,
  then accepts a right one. `create t int; insert; drop; create T text` in one
  transaction refuses `1` against the new TEXT column, and a blob table
  recreated in a transaction refuses `1.5`. Replay on an empty cache matches in
  each case.
  2. **Order of errors.** It holds on every path: text and arguments, then names
  (`buildSink`'s lookups), then argument too big (`checkLength` in
  `buildSink`), then readonly (the read-only branch returns before `check`),
  then type (`check`), then UNIQUE, then record too big (`run`).
  `TestEngine_tooBig` pins it against SQLite.
     - Confirmed: in a read-only transaction, both a wrong type and a right type
  get `attempt to write a readonly database`.
     - UNIQUE against record too big cannot collide in a one-column table,
  because a stored key's record already fits.
     - A literal longer than the limit is not compared with SQLite. The README
  records this, and the parser's `SQLITE_LIMIT_SQL_LENGTH` check covers the
  default limit.
  3. **`fromCore` and `core.ErrTooBig`.** The core returns ErrTooBig from
  `checkRow` and from the upsert before any `tx.fail` or `touch`, so the
  transaction goes on. The engine then rolls back to the savepoint, or rolls
  back in autocommit. The engine test "keep a transaction usable after a value
  that is too long" covers this through commit and restart.
  4. **The driver.**
     - Confirmed by running: `float32` NaN, `sql.NullString{}`, a pointer to a
  nil `[]byte`, text with NUL, and invalid UTF-8 each get s4ql's own refusal.
  `-0.0` gives UNIQUE against `0.0`, and reads back as `+0` after replay.
     - Named types, pointers and `driver.Valuer` follow
  `DefaultParameterConverter`, as database/sql's default does.
     - Read side: `value()` copies each blob, and the binding's `ColumnBlob`
  already copies with `C.GoBytes`, so the application never aliases cache
  memory.
     - Write side: the application's `[]byte` is not copied, but nothing keeps
  it after the call returns. `BindBlob` copies, the annotation is marshalled to
  the spool at once, and the commit object is read back from the cache.
  Confirmed: insert a buffer, change it, insert it again in one transaction,
  commit, replay; the result is `[aa bb]`.
  5. **`core.Options.LengthLimit`.** Nothing outside tests can set it. `core` is
  internal, `s4ql.go` passes `core.Options{}`, `OpenStore` lives in
  `export_test.go`, and `internal/simulator` leaves the limit at its default.
  Two instances with different limits could disagree on replay only on paper. A
  one-line note that the limit must be the same for every instance of a database
  would be enough.
  6. **The test rules.** None excuses more than the agreed rules allow.
     - `pair.checkType` excuses a call only when the engine's error is exactly
  the expected message, and only after SQLite has prepared the statement, so
  name errors are still compared. A readonly or too-big result where the rule
  expects "not supported" shows up as a failure, not an excuse.
     - In drivercheck, `checkRefused` now requires every run to fail with the
  expected message. `typeRefusal` returns "" in a read-only transaction and when
  SQLite cannot prepare, which also fails rather than excuses.
     - `compare` fails any call that s4ql ran with an argument that `reaches`
  says it should refuse, so a value s4ql should have refused but accepted cannot
  pass unseen either.

  Nits, none blocking:
  - `conn.go`, the `CheckNamedValue` doc comment: one line ("…what it points to.
  It never fails, …") was not rewrapped after the edit.
  - `rows.go`, `value`: the extra blob copy is defensive only, since
  `ColumnBlob` already returns fresh bytes. It doubles memory for a large blob,
  and database/sql copies again on `Scan` into `*[]byte`. Keeping it is fine.

  - **Generator, `stmttest`.** A new file `values.go` holds random values and

  transaction must pass `typeRule`, which came from the review: such an insert
  never reaches the log if the transaction does not commit.

  core. The checks did turn up gaps in the test code itself:

  ends. A later `drop table` in the same transaction then got `6/6: database
  table is locked` (seeds 103,080 and 107,735 of a throwaway 1,500-seed run).
  SQLite's side now prepares such a query inside the transaction.
  - `Sequence.Like` turned a rejected `+Inf` argument into a valid real on later
  runs of a prepared statement. It now keeps rejected values.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The statement pass now reads the statements of slice 5. The branch is
  `columns-stmt`, five commits on `origin/main` f842ffb (#71 merged;
  `types-checks` had the same tree). `make test`, `make lint` and `make
  check-browser` pass, the full runs of `./internal/sql/...`,
  `./internal/engine/` and crosscheck pass, and the new tests pass with
  `-count=20` and with `-race`. Fuzzing found nothing (FuzzGenerate 60 s,
  FuzzLiteral 46 s, FuzzCheck 45 s). Nothing is pushed.

  **Shape of the statement types** (`internal/sql/stmt/stmt.go`)
  - `CreateTable{Span, Table, Columns []Column}`, where `Column{Name, Type,
  PrimaryKey, NotNull}`, with `Key()` and `ColumnsError() *sqlerr.Error`.
  - `Insert{Span, Table, Columns []Name, Values []Term}`. `Columns` is nil when
  there is no column list; `Term` is `{Value, Parameter, Span}`. `Parameters()`
  counts the `?`, and the k-th `?` has number k.
  - `Select{Span, Table, Columns []Name}`. `Columns` is nil for `*`, and repeats
  are kept.
  - Names compare by `Name.Fold` (ASCII letters only, as in SQLite).

  **One decision to confirm.** I read the brief's "follow the pass's existing
  rule" for a column named twice, or more than 2000 columns, against SQLite and
  kept SQLite's order. SQLite raises both while it parses, but only after
  `sqlite3StartTable` has looked for the table. If the table exists, SQLite
  gives `table t1 already exists` instead. So the answer depends on the
  database, and the spec accepts that kind of difference only for syntax errors.
  The pass therefore supports such a statement, and `ColumnsError()` gives
  SQLite's error: `too many columns on t` at the 2001st column (checked first),
  else `duplicate column name: <later column>`. The engine should give it after
  its check of the table. The tests check it against SQLite with and without the
  table. If you want the pass to give the error itself as on an empty database,
  that needs a new accepted difference.

  **Other decisions of detail** (in `docs/todo.md` and the README of the pass)
  - Not supported, with new names: `column without PRIMARY KEY or NOT NULL`,
  `column without NOT NULL`, `second PRIMARY KEY`, `table without PRIMARY KEY`,
  `column named twice`, `* and another result column`, `result column other than
  a column or *`, `parameter beyond SQLite's limit` (the 32,767th `?`; SQLite
  gives `too many SQL variables` while it parses).
  - `select true from t` is `TRUE or FALSE`; `select "true"` is a column. An
  alias in the select list is `alias`.
  - `insert into t default values` is now named `DEFAULT VALUES`.
  - NOT NULL on the primary key is kept as written; SQLite records the same
  table with or without it.

  **Pinned numbers that moved** (pass only; the engine and driver numbers did
  not move)
  - The pass's tests set `stmttest.Generator.Columns = 3`, so the generated
  texts are different.
  - Mutants: syntax errors where SQLite does not say went 0 → 85. 83 are
  duplicate-column tables with a later syntax error, which is the known gap. Not
  supported where SQLite does not say went 343 → 310.
  - Near misses: 956 → 3022. 2080 of these are the four new forms that SQLite
  refuses with `table "t" has more than one primary key`.
  - Corpus: supported texts went 1,198 → 12,736, all checked in SQLite; its
  excused counts did not move.

  **For the engine builder**
  - `checkColumns` in `internal/engine/errors.go` refuses:
    - a table of more than one column;
    - a column that says NOT NULL;
    - an insert with no column list, or with more than one column or value;
    - a select that names columns.
  - It is called from `engine.Check` and `prepare`, and `NewPlan` panics for
  these forms. The driver's `check` in `conn.go` calls `engine.Check`, so
  `Prepare` refuses them.
  - The engine and driver tests use `checkText` wrappers that treat this refusal
  as "not supported". Remove all three, then set `Generator.Columns` in your
  tests. With `Columns` at 0 the generator draws exactly as before: I compared a
  27,000-line dump from the old and the new code.
  - SQLite facts the engine needs:
    - A result column is named as the table stores it: `select B` from a column
  `b` gives `b`.
    - For a name in double quotes, the error is `no such column: "v" - should
  this be a string literal in single-quotes?`.
    - The other errors are `N values for M columns`, `table t has N columns but
  M values were supplied`, `table t has no column named x`, and `NOT NULL
  constraint failed: t.c`.

  **Where a reviewer should look**
  - `ColumnsError` against `sqlite3AddColumn`.
  - The guards in `createTable` and `column` in `recognize.go`.
  - `checkCreateTable`, `checkInsert` and `checkSelect` in `stmt_test.go`.
  - The sections of `internal/sql/stmt/README.md` that a writer sub-agent
  rewrote; I reviewed its diff.

  **Self-review.** The review found five nits.
  - Fixed: the parameter count was quadratic in a long row; two comments were
  wrong.
  - Documented: a duplicate column together with a second PRIMARY KEY gives "not
  supported", where SQLite would give `duplicate column name`.
  - Left: `Prepare` parses a supported text twice until the engine runs slice 5,
  as slice 4's pass did.

  Mutation testing of the slice 5 code and long fuzz runs are not done; the
  mutation tester's own test passes and no keys moved. The diary is at
  `docs/diary/2026-11-20-columns-stmt.md`, two steps.

✻ Churned for 3h 24m 15s · done Friday 1:45 AM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Slice 5 now runs end to end in the engine and the `database/sql` driver. `make
  test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser` pass. The work is on branch
  `columns-engine` (8 commits on top of `origin/columns-stmt` at f9cc7be), not
  pushed. Worktree:
  /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a41e1ff7518ebea96. The
  new tests also pass with `-count=20`, and under `-race` (`-count=20` for the
  engine and the root package, `-count=5` for drivercheck).

  **What I built**
  - **Create table:** one or more columns, with the key at any position.
  - **Insert:** with or without a list of columns. Each value goes to the
  position of its column, and the key is read at its position.
  - **Select:** named columns in any order, with repeats, or `*`. Result column
  names are as the table stores them.
  - **Removed:** `checkColumns` and both `checkText` wrappers. The driver's
  `check` calls `stmt.Check` alone again, so a supported text is parsed once at
  Prepare. Prepare still refuses at once what the text alone shows is not
  supported (tested for a column without NOT NULL and a column named twice).
  - **New error constant:** `sqlerr.CodeConstraintNotNull` (1299).
  - **Telemetry:** no event carried anything per column or per value, so nothing
  changed.
  - **Annotations:** hold every argument, and replay into an empty cache gives
  the same state (tested).

  **Order of errors (read in sqlite3.c, run in the 3.54.1 shell, pinned in
  tests)**
  - **Insert:**
    1. Errors of the text and of the arguments.
    2. `no such table`.
    3. `table t has no column named x`, in the order of the list.
    4. Number of values: `table t has N columns but M values were supplied`
  without a list, `M values for N columns` with one.
    5. `string or blob too big` for a value (SQLite gives it at bind).
    6. `attempt to write a readonly database` in a read-only transaction.
    7. `NOT NULL constraint failed: t.c` (19/1299) for the first column without
  a value in table order, the key included.
    8. "not supported" for a value of another type, naming the first such column
  in table order (SQLite's type check comes here).
    9. `UNIQUE constraint failed`.
    10. `string or blob too big` for a record that is too long.
  - **Rule of slice 4 kept:** NOT NULL comes before the type refusal because
  SQLite tests every NOT NULL before it looks at a type. A column left out plus
  a wrong type in another column gives NOT NULL in SQLite too.
  - **Create table:** `already exists` first, then `ColumnsError()`. Both come
  before the read-only refusal.
  - **Select:** `no such table`, then `no such column: x` for the first unknown
  name. A name in double quotes gives `no such column: "x" - should this be a
  string literal in single-quotes?`.

  **Decisions of detail** (in `docs/todo.md` for you to confirm; one bullet
  added to slice 5 in the roadmap on the order of insert errors)
  - NOT NULL sets the count of changes to 0, as UNIQUE does. The type refusal
  leaves it. `checked` in `engine.go` decides this.
  - The plan holds no NOT NULL. A table with `not null` on its key and one
  without give the same plan, commit object and cache table. Only SQLite's
  schema text differs, and s4ql does not expose it.
  - The plan format prints `insert into t` when there is no list, and `project
  a, b` for named columns.

  **Pinned numbers that moved** — the generator draws exactly as before. The
  engine now runs two texts of `stmttest.Others` that it used to refuse: `insert
  into %v values (%v)` and `select %v from %v`.
  - Engine `TestDifferential`: excused type refusals 363 → 371.
  - drivercheck `TestGenerated`: excusedPrepare 1010 → 1012, excusedType 173 →
  180, excusedRowsOpen 37 → 38, excusedOutside 48 → 49.

  **Tests**
  - **Engine:**
    - `TestEngine_columns` compares 13 shapes with SQLite: two- and three-column
  tables of mixed types, key at each position, about 45 statements each. It
  checks every answer (codes, message, column names, types, row order), the
  count of changes, the tables, and the tables after a replay. It also covers a
  2000-column table.
    - 17 new pinned cases in `TestEngine_errors`.
    - New cases in `TestEngine_tooBig`, `TestEngine_annotations`,
  `TestConn_Begin` (write, roll back, read-only) and `TestBuild`.
  - **Driver:** `TestColumns` in `conn_test.go`. In drivercheck, `TestColumns`
  runs the same shapes through every call against mattn, pinned and through the
  pool, plus four hand-written sequences.

  **Self-review** (code-review high): no bugs in the order of errors; six nits.
  Fixed:
  - The name lookups were quadratic for wide tables; they now use `positionsOf`.
  - The differential test and the simulation accepted "not supported" for the
  two supported texts of `Others`. They now fail it.
  - Three comments.

  Two other fixes:
  - `make test` caught three stale equivalent mutants in
  `internal/sql/mutation/equivalent.txt`; removed.
  - `Call.Supported` stays false for those two texts, because `Sequence.next`
  draws a random number only for supported calls. Changing the flag would move
  every pinned number.

  **Left for the builder of the generated checks**
  - Set `Generator.Columns` in the engine's and drivercheck's generated tests
  and the simulation. Planted bugs, mutation testing and fuzz targets are also
  left.
  - The type-rule helpers read only `Values[0]` and the key column: `typeRule`,
  `valueKind`, `insertType`, `oracle.column`, `pair.reach` and
  `pair.checkLength` in `differential_test.go`, and `typeRefusal`, `reach` and
  `columnType` in drivercheck. They must read every value and expect the first
  wrong column in table order, after NOT NULL.
  - mattn's SQLite reads `"x"` as a string (DQS on), while the pinned build
  gives `no such column`. A select of a double-quoted unknown name cannot be
  compared with mattn.

  **Where a reviewer should look**
  - `buildSink` and `insert.check` in `/internal/engine/operator.go`: the order
  of the checks.
  - `checked` in `/internal/engine/engine.go`.
  - `shape.steps` in `/internal/engine/engine_test.go`: what each step is meant
  to reach.
  - The supported-text conditions in `pair.step`
  (`/internal/engine/differential_test.go`) and in
  `/internal/engine/simulation_test.go`.

  its diff.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Verdict: merge as is.** I found one error that comes out in a different
  order from SQLite. It never stores or returns a wrong value. Fix it after the
  merge, or first if you want strict parity with SQLite's errors.

  `go test -short ./internal/engine/ .` passes. I wrote throwaway tests against
  the oracle (about 50 statements, including two-fault combinations), ran them,
  and deleted them. The worktree is clean.

  **Finding 1 (low): "too big" for the record comes after UNIQUE, but SQLite
  gives it first.**
  - **Where:** `insert.run` in `internal/engine/operator.go`. Also
  `docs/roadmap.md`, slice 5, which says "A key that exists comes last".
  - **Scenario:** limit 1000, `create table m (k int primary key, a text not
  null, b blob not null) strict, without rowid`, then `insert into m values (1,
  'x', x'01')`. Next, `insert into m values (?, ?, ?)` with 1, 600 bytes of text
  and 600 bytes of blob. Each value fits the limit, but the row does not.
  - **What differs:** SQLite gives `18/18: string or blob too big`. The engine
  gives `19/1555: UNIQUE constraint failed: m.k`. The same happens with a column
  list in another order. Confirmed by running it.
  - **Why:** for a WITHOUT ROWID table, SQLite builds the record of the whole
  row (OP_MakeRecord) before it checks the key. The engine calls `get` for the
  key before the core's `Upsert` measures the row.
  - **Impact:** both fail, nothing is written, and the count of changes is 0 on
  both sides. Only the code and message differ. This could not happen in slice
  4: with one column, a row whose key exists has the same size as a row that
  already fit. At the default limit of 1,000,000,000 bytes, an application needs
  a row of over 1 GB whose key already exists.
  - **Fix:** check the size of the record before the `get` of the key. Then
  correct the roadmap and the doc of `Conn.Exec`, which repeat the wrong order.

  **Checked against SQLite, and it matches (confirmed by running unless
  noted):**
  1. **Values reach the right column:** with the key first, in the middle, or
  last; with no column list; with lists in any order and names in any case,
  quoted or not; with `?` arguments numbered across the values. The stored rows
  equal SQLite's, and UNIQUE names the key column at any position. A prepared
  statement parses its text again on every run (`prepared` in `conn.go`), so a
  dropped and recreated table cannot reuse old positions (read in the code, not
  run).
  2. **Select:** repeats, any order, `*`, names in quotes or another case, and a
  column named `rowid` or `oid`. `rowid` and `_rowid_` on a WITHOUT ROWID table
  give `no such column`, as in SQLite. The result names are the stored names,
  which is also SQLite's behaviour.
  3. **Order of insert errors:**
     - An unknown column comes before the wrong number of values, and the first
  one in the list is reported.
     - The number of values comes before an argument that is too long, which
  comes before read-only.
     - For a missing column, the first in table order is reported, also when the
  key is the one missing in the middle.
     - NOT NULL comes before a value of the wrong type, also when SQLite's own
  type error is on an earlier column.
     - A wrong type comes before UNIQUE.
     - Unknown column, wrong count, and NOT NULL all come before UNIQUE.
  4. **`checked` and the count of changes:** NOT NULL and UNIQUE set it to 0.
  Errors of the names or the count leave it, inside and outside a write
  transaction. "Not supported" leaves it, which follows the rule.
  5. **Create table:** `already exists` comes before a duplicate column and
  before more than 2,000 columns, and all of these come before read-only. A key
  with and without `not null`, at any position, gives the same table as SQLite.
  6. **Driver:** `Rows.Columns` takes the engine's names. `value()` copies each
  blob per value, so `select b, b` gives two separate slices (read in the code).
  7. **Replay:** on an empty instance, the log gives the same state. I checked
  this with the key third, blob, real and integer columns, -0.0, 1e300, and
  quoted names.
  8. **Test excuses:** `pair.step` and the simulation now fail every "not
  supported" of a statement that the pass supports, except the refusals for
  arguments, length, and type, which are checked against the rule. `typeRule`
  and `valueKind` still assume one column and the key (`Values[0]`). On a table
  of several columns that would show up as a false failure, never a false
  excuse. The generators do not make such tables yet, so nothing is hidden.

  **Gap, not a bug:** the generated differential tests and the simulation run no
  tables of several columns. Slice 5 is covered only by the pinned tests in
  `engine_test.go` and `drivercheck` (including `TestColumns`).

⏺ Agent "Mutation and fuzz for types" is waiting on its own background work · 2h
  47m 22s

› Message from general-purpose
  Verdict: merge as is. I found no bug that can corrupt or lose data. One
  optional simplification is listed at the end.

  `go test -short ./internal/core/ ./internal/engine/` passes (core 78s, engine
  15s). I wrote a throwaway test in `internal/core`, ran it, and deleted it.
  Nothing on the branch was changed or pushed.

  **1. `core.Tx.Insert` on its own (internal/core/tx.go). Correct. I confirmed
  this by committing and replaying.**
  - The throwaway test inserted into a table created in the same transaction and
  into one that already existed. It covered delete-then-Insert of the same key,
  `Savepoint` + Insert + `RollbackTo`, and `Savepoint` + Insert + `Release`. It
  also tried -0.0 against an existing 0.0 key, and NaN, NULL key, wrong type,
  wrong arity and an unknown table.
  - Then it closed the instance and replayed the log into an empty cache "b".
  The replayed state was equal.
  - Commit objects:
    - Delete+Insert of key 1 became `upsert t 1,11`. The first record of
  "existed" stays, because `touch` uses `insert or ignore`.
    - The row rolled back to the savepoint is absent.
    - The row of the created table is in the create's commit.
  - It passes `touch(t, key, false)` for a new key. That is right: `or ignore`
  inserts only when no row has the key, and a key not yet touched has its state
  from the start of the transaction.
  - It runs `check`, `table` and `checkRow` exactly as Upsert does, so the
  checks of exact types, NaN/Inf, NUL, UTF-8 and length are the same.
  - -0.0 is handled as Upsert handles it (raw key into the temp table, stored as
  0.0). Confirmed: Insert(-0.0) next to an existing 0.0 returns `false, nil`.
  - After a failed transaction it gives the failure error (confirmed). There is
  no read-only core `Tx`, so that case does not arise.
  - `or ignore` cannot hide other constraints. The cache tables only have PK
  conflicts (non-key columns allow NULL), and a NULL key is refused before
  SQLite sees it.

  **2. The engine's use (internal/engine/operator.go, `insert.run`). Correct.**
  - Paths I traced where Insert could still insert after `get` found the key:
    - Same transaction, same connection and snapshot: nothing changes between
  `get` and Insert.
    - The statement's own earlier rows are already in the table, so `get` sees
  them and Insert agrees.
    - 0.0 / -0.0 is the same key to both calls (confirmed above).
    - Integer vs real keys cannot mix, because `checkType` refuses them in a
  STRICT table.
    - Text compares with BINARY in both calls.
  - None of these lets it insert after all. If it ever did, the engine returns
  `internalError`, and the statement's `rollbackTo` (tx.go:335) or the
  autocommit rollback (engine.go:370) removes the row. So even that path loses
  nothing.
  - `readOnly.insertNew` returning `false, nil` is reached only through the
  planted bug `checkOutside`, where it gives the unique error without measuring.
  That is harmless.

  **3. Shape.** I would keep `Tx.Insert`.
  - It is SQLite's own INSERT semantics. It measures exactly, whereas a Go
  estimate or a measure-only function cannot be exact without trying the write.
  - It reuses `touch`, `checkRow` and `tooBig` unchanged.
  - Having `Upsert` refuse too big first does not work: it replaces the row for
  a key that exists, and a fitting row with an existing key must not be written.
  - Optional follow-up, not blocking: the engine could call `Insert` alone for
  every row and drop `get` and `upsert` from the insert path. `false` would mean
  `UNIQUE constraint failed` and `ErrTooBig` would mean too big, which gives
  SQLite's order with one round trip.
    - It would also make `Tx.Insert` the main write path, so the simulation and
  the differential tests drive it. Today it runs only for duplicate keys;
  ad08b47 itself notes that the core's tests do not drive it.
    - `upsert` would stay for the planted bug `skipUnique`.

  **4. Tests. They pin what they claim. Minor gaps only.**
  - `TestTx_tooBig` new subtest:
    - It checks against SQLite's own table of the same shape, at each length
  from limit-20 to limit+2, for both an existing key and a new key. So the
  boundary is exact.
    - It requires both a row that is taken and more than two refused.
    - It compares the commit object with the model's net effect, which shows
  that a refused new key leaves no touched record and that the transaction goes
  on after `ErrTooBig`.
    - Gaps: it does not replay into a fresh cache (my throwaway test did, and it
  passed). The two refused-branch `is.True(t, !inserted)` calls have no
  message.
  - `TestEngine_tooBig` new subtest: compares error, changes and final state
  with SQLite in autocommit and in a transaction, for both column orders and for
  a new key, and requires that both outcomes occur.
  - `TestInsert` and `TestConn_writeIn` pin the order of calls for an existing
  key (`get`, then `insert new`, then rollback) and for a new key (`get`, then
  `upsert`, with no `insert new`), and that the error is passed on.
  - The `inserted == true` → `internalError` branch is never driven by a test.
  It is unreachable with the fakes.

  a bool on `Term`: code that reads a nil `Value` as a parameter would then take
  a NULL for one.

  **Decisions of detail** (recorded in `docs/todo.md`; the roadmap's slice 6
  gained three "rejected" bullets):
  - `-null` and `+null` give `NULL after a sign`. For `-null` the offset is at
  the NULL, as for a string after a sign; for `+null` it is at the `+`. `-
  -null` stays `second sign` and `+-null` stays `+ sign`.
  - `v2 text null` gives the new name `NULL constraint`, also next to NOT NULL
  and on the key. `default null` stays `DEFAULT`.
  - `is null` and `is not null` give `IS`; `isnull`, `notnull` and `x not null`
  give `NULL test`. `select null` and NULL in WHERE or LIMIT keep their existing
  names.

  5; `Sequence` leaves it at 0.
  - With `Nulls` at 0 the generator draws exactly as before. A dump of 27,000
  texts and arguments of `Generator` and `Sequence` from 30 seeds matched the
  old code byte for byte.

  other numbers with `Nulls` on. Grouping the excused texts by SQLite's message

  messages are the same.

  statements are CREATE TABLE where there were 520.

✻ Crunched for 17s · done Friday 3:55 AM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Branch `null-core` (6 commits on top of `origin/main` 4e42473), in worktree
  /Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-aaa5971f012b90c9b. It
  is not pushed. It merges cleanly with the newer `origin/main` (caba023, the
  statement pass of slice 6). `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser`
  pass. The new and changed tests pass with `-count=20`, also under `-race`. The
  diary is at docs/diary/2026-11-20-null-core.md, in 3 steps.

  **What I built**
  - **Format:**
    - `Column` gets `bool nullable = 3` (regenerated with `make generate`).
    - The writer and reader refuse a create table whose key holds a nullable
  column, and a NULL in a `DeleteRows` key.
    - They cannot refuse a NULL in an `UpsertRows` row, for a column that does
  not take NULL or for the key, because the batch does not carry its table. The
  core refuses those in a write and at replay, in `table.checkValue`.
    - The samples mark `users.age` and `kv.c` as nullable; those two already
  held NULL. `FuzzCommitWriter` now makes some columns nullable. The format
  README, `docs/todo.md` (as decided by the lead, for Markus to confirm) and the
  slice 6 sentence of the roadmap are updated.
  - **Core:**
    - `column.nullable` is carried through `newTable`, `createTable`, the cache,
  `load`, the commit object and replay.
    - `Upsert` and `Insert` refuse NULL with `ErrInvalid` in any column that is
  not nullable and in the key, before SQLite sees the row. `Delete` and `Get`
  refuse a NULL key. A NULL passes the exact-type check for a nullable column of
  any type.
    - `Tx.CreateTable` now commits the table as the cache gives it back, so a
  `nullable: false` from the caller is not committed.

  **How the cache records it, and caches that exist**
  - A nullable column is declared `default null`. Every other column outside the
  key is declared `not null`, so SQLite also refuses a NULL there.
  - `conn.load` reads the default from `pragma_table_info`. Any other default,
  or `not null` together with `default null`, is refused with `ErrCacheVersion`.
  The default is the only free channel: a v1 cache has no `not null` on any
  column, so that flag cannot tell its columns from nullable ones.
  - The cache version goes from 1 to 2. The old reader would read `default null`
  as a column that does not take NULL, silently; with 2 it refuses the cache.
  - A v1 cache is raised to v2 in place on open, after every other check. A
  refused cache, such as one of another database, keeps version 1. A v1 cache
  that holds NULL outside its key is refused (the old core accepted such NULLs;
  no engine wrote them).
  - `Tx.Insert` now uses `insert ... on conflict do nothing`. `insert or ignore`
  would also skip a row that breaks `not null`.

  **Decisions of detail** (recorded in docs/todo.md and internal/core/README.md)
  - A log or cache from before this change that holds NULL in a column without
  `nullable` is now refused: as a broken log at replay, or as `ErrCacheVersion`
  when the cache opens. Only tests and the old samples wrote such NULLs.

  **Planted bug `nullZero`:** the commit object writes each NULL as the zero
  value of its column's type, while the cache keeps the NULL. Over 200 seeds it
  was found in 179, and in none of the 200 runs without the bug. By symptom:
  "with the changes" 179, "a read saw at commit" 177, "the cache of" 124. The
  default run finds it 4 times.

  **Tests**
  - `TestTx_null` is new.
  - `TestTx_types` adds a nullable column of every type.
  - `TestTx_tooBig` adds two shapes with NULL, and the boundary stays exact
  against SQLite.
  - `TestTx_Savepoint_random` adds nullable shapes and NULL refusals, with new
  minimums of 50 and 14.
  - The simulation:
    - 5 of its 8 shapes have nullable columns.
    - 1 upsert in 10 puts NULL where a column takes none and must get
  `ErrInvalid`.
    - Two trace markers are added, "upserts with NULL" and "refused upserts of
  NULL", with a minimum of a quarter of what each workload reached.
  - The model's strict replay refuses a value that does not fit its column, and
  `cacheDump` reads `default null` with its own SQL.

  **Run times:** `go test -count=1 -v ./internal/core` took 57.0 s before and
  60.9 s after. `TestTx_Savepoint_random` went from 2.32 s to 2.28 s,
  `TestTx_tooBig` from 0.04 s to 0.05 s, and `TestTx_types` stayed at 0.02 s.

  **For the engine builder**
  - No signature changed, and `internal/engine` is untouched. Its tests pass.
  - To make a column take NULL, set `Nullable: new(true)` on its
  `s4qlpb.Column`. Never set it on a key column; the format refuses that.
  - The core returns `ErrInvalid` for a NULL in a column that does not take
  NULL. The engine must give SQLite's "NOT NULL constraint failed" first, in
  SQLite's order.
  - Null comes back from `Get` and `Scan` as `Value{Null}`.

  **Where a reviewer should look**
  - internal/core/cache.go: `setUp`, `positionAt`, `upgrade`, `checkNoNull`,
  `load`, `createSQL`, `checkValue`, `insert`.
  - internal/format/check.go.
  - internal/core/tx.go: `committed`, `zeroValue`, `CreateTable`.
  - The new cache bullet in internal/core/README.md.

  **Review findings left alone**
  - `load` does not require `not null` on the non-key columns of v2 tables,
  because tables carried over from v1 lack it. The README says so.
  - The reviewer flagged the roadmap edit, made before Markus confirmed the
  field. You asked for that edit, and the todo marks the field for Markus to
  confirm.

  Created PR #77 (ctrl+o to expand)

✻ Churned for 18s · done Friday 4:08 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  Verdict: fix one small thing first, then merge. I found nothing that can
  corrupt, lose or silently misread data. The one real bug makes a start fail
  when it should not. It is cheap to fix and has a concrete repro.

  `go test -short ./internal/core/ ./internal/format/` passes. I wrote throwaway
  tests in `internal/core/zzreview_test.go`, ran them, and deleted the file;
  the worktree is clean and nothing was pushed.

  **Medium: a start fails at random when instances open one version 1 cache at
  once** (confirmed by running it)
  - Where: `conn.setUp` in `internal/core/cache.go`.
  - Cause: `old` is read with `count(... where version = 1)`, then
  `positionAt(1)` reads the version again. Each is a separate autocommit read.
  If another instance runs `upgrade` between the two reads, `positionAt(1)`
  finds version 2 and fails.
  - Result: `ErrCacheVersion` with the message "it has version 2, and this
  version of s4ql reads version 2; open the database with a new cache, or delete
  this one". The start fails loudly and the message tells the operator to
  delete a cache that is fine. Since S3 is the truth, no data is lost, but the
  error is wrong.
  - Repro: 20 rounds of 8 concurrent `core.Open` calls on a version 1 cache
  built with the old statements. Rounds 1 and 18 each had one instance fail this
  way.
  - Fix: in setUp, let `positionAt` accept version 1 or `cacheVersion`
  (`upgrade` already does `update ... where version = 1`, so a second upgrade is
  harmless). Or re-read the version inside `upgrade`'s `begin immediate`. Then
  add this concurrent test.

  **Low, no fix needed**
  - **Atomicity:** `upgrade` checks and bumps the version in one `begin
  immediate` transaction, so a crash leaves version 1 or version 2, never a mix.
  A cache refused for another reason keeps version 1, and a test pins this.
  - **Tables carried over from version 1** keep their old declarations: no `not
  null`, no default. `load` reads them as columns that do not take NULL, also
  after a restart and beside new tables; the existing test shows this. SQLite
  itself would accept a NULL there (I confirmed that a raw insert works). But
  every write path checks first: Upsert and Insert call `checkRow`, and replay
  calls `checkValue` on every value, both full and partial. So nothing can store
  a NULL there. The only cost is that SQLite gives no second line of defence on
  those tables, and their schema text differs from a rebuilt cache's. Nothing
  at runtime compares schema text.
  - **Default null as the record of nullability** (confirmed): a table with all
  five types and odd column names (`a"b`, `default`, `null`, `not null`,
  `default null`, `dflt_value`, `'x'`, `x)`, `smør 🥪`) and a table name with
  quotes in it round-tripped exactly through `Tx.Table`, a restart of the same
  cache, and a replay into an empty cache.
    - SQLite reports "no default" as NULL and `default null` as the text "null";
  `load` refuses any other default and a key or `notnull` column that has one.
    - No core statement relies on a default: upsert, insert and replay all list
  every column, and a partial replay only updates.
    - `insert or replace` replaces a NULL with the default only in a `not null`
  column, and no such column has a default.
  - **Old logs and the engine of `main`:** an old `CreateTable` has no
  `nullable` field, so `GetNullable()` is false. The engine of `main` refuses
  NULL arguments, the literal NULL, and columns that take NULL (`checkNulls`,
  `checkArgument`), so it never wrote a NULL. Only direct calls to the old core
  could have, and the formats are still drafts at version 0.
  - **A version 2 cache opened by `main`:** `main`'s `position()` requires
  version 1 and refuses with `ErrCacheVersion` (read in the code). A `main`
  instance already running on a shared cache when a new one upgrades it fails at
  its next `position()`: loud, not a misread. The reverse holds too: `main`'s
  reader refuses field 3 of `Column` as an unknown field.
  - **`Tx.CreateTable`:** `newTable(ct).createTable()` keeps the name, the
  columns in order, the types, `nullable` and the key order. The writer has
  already refused unknown fields and a key column that takes NULL. The only
  difference is that it leaves out a `nullable` set explicitly to false, which
  is what the change intends.
  - **`on conflict do nothing`:** the core refuses a NULL and a wrong type
  before SQLite sees the row. So a `not null` failure from SQLite could only
  come from an internal fault, and it goes to `tx.fail` instead of being
  swallowed as "not inserted", which is better than `insert or ignore`. On an
  existing key, a row with NULLs that is too big still gets `ErrTooBig`
  (confirmed: limit 1000, refused from a blob of 996 bytes), and a fitting row
  still gives `inserted=false`.
  - **Format checks:** the writer and reader share
  `checkChange`/`checkCreateTable`/`checkPrimaryKey`, so a NULL in a key and a
  key column that takes NULL are refused on both sides. The samples and the
  unknown field number (moved to 4) are updated.
  - **Cost:** `checkNoNull` scans the whole table once per non-key column while
  holding the write lock. It runs once per cache, at the upgrade.

  **Tests:** `TestTx_null`, `TestTx_types`, `TestTx_tooBig` (with NULL rows
  checked against SQLite's own table), `TestTx_Savepoint_random`, the simulation
  shapes and its minimums, and the planted bug `nullZero` do test what their
  names say. There is a real version 1 test: a cache made with the old
  statements and holding rows is upgraded, refuses a NULL, gets a new nullable
  table, restarts, and is compared with a replay. A second test refuses a
  version 1 cache that holds a NULL. What is missing: a concurrent open of a
  version 1 cache (see the bug above), and a version 1 cache with non-int types.

  Slice 6 (`null`) now runs end to end in the engine and through `database/sql`.

  **What I built**
  - **Table definition:** the plan's `ColumnDef` has `Nullable`: not the key,
  and no `not null`. `Format` prints ` NULL` after the type, so old plans print
  as before. `buildSink` sets `nullable` on the commit column only when it is
  true, and a nullable key is an internal error. The literal NULL is a new
  `*Null` expression. A replay gives the same table.
  - **NULL as a value:** `buildValues` gives NULL to a column the list leaves
  out. `checkArgument` accepts NULL. The driver turns nil into NULL. Reading
  gives nil, which scans into `sql.Null*`, a pointer or `any`. A scan into a
  plain `string` or `int64` gets `database/sql`'s own error. The annotation
  keeps the nil argument as NULL.

  shows, such as `-null`.

  `TestNulls`)**
  - nil, which becomes NULL, for: `nil`, any nil pointer (`(*[]byte)(nil)` too),
  a `Valuer` that gives nil (`sql.NullString{}`, `sql.Null[[]byte]{}`), and a
  nil pointer to a value-receiver `Valuer`.
  - A nil `[]byte`, which stays `argument N of type []byte, which is nil`, for:
  `[]byte(nil)`, `new([]byte)`, `sql.Null[[]byte]{Valid: true}` with nil `V`,
  and `sql.RawBytes(nil)`.

  too-long value and the read-only refusal. It comes before the type refusal, a
  record that is too big, and UNIQUE.

  refusal.

  **`insert.run`**

✻ Churned for 51s · done Friday 6:02 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  Verdict: merge as is. I found nothing that can store or return a wrong value,
  or that differs from SQLite, in any case an application can reach today.

  **What I ran**
  - `go test -short ./internal/engine/ .` passes on origin/null-engine (engine
  11.2s, root 4.4s).
  - I wrote throwaway tests (now deleted, worktree clean) that compare SQLite
  with the engine through `pair.step` and `pinTx`. Each step checks the answer
  and the count of changes. Each script checks the table state at the end and
  again after a replay into an empty cache. All passed:
    - **Faults, one at a time and in pairs:** a column left out next to a value
  of the wrong type; NULL for a `not null` column next to a value of the wrong
  type in the key; NULL for the key next to a value of the wrong type elsewhere;
  NULL as a literal and as an argument at every position, key included, with
  the key first and in the middle; two NOT NULL faults (SQLite names the first
  in table order); a key that exists plus NULL in a `not null` column (NOT NULL
  wins); a key that exists plus a value of the wrong type (not supported, as
  agreed in slice 4); an unknown column plus NULL; the wrong number of values
  plus NULL. In every case the error and the count of changes matched SQLite.
    - **Length limit of 1000 bytes:** an argument over the limit next to NULL in
  a `not null` column gives too big, as SQLite does when it binds. A record
  that is too long next to NULL in a `not null` column gives NOT NULL first. A
  record that is too long with a key that exists gives too big before UNIQUE. A
  row that fits because of a NULL, inserted twice, gives UNIQUE.
    - **Keys:** 0.0 against -0.0 as a literal and as an argument, x'' twice
  (literal, then an empty `[]byte`), INT64 min twice, and 'a' against 'A'
  against 'a '. Each gave UNIQUE exactly when SQLite does.
    - **In a transaction:** UNIQUE against the transaction's own earlier inserts
  and against rows from before it; an insert that works after a statement
  failed and was rolled back to its savepoint; `drop table` and `create table`
  with the same name in one transaction, then the same keys again; a rollback.
  The state and the replayed log matched SQLite after each.
  - **Driver:** a `Valuer` that returns a typed nil pointer gets "not supported
  … which database/sql cannot convert". One that returns `[]byte(nil)` gets
  "argument 2 of type []byte, which is nil". A nil pointer to a `Valuer` with a
  pointer receiver has its `Value` called on the nil pointer and stores what it
  returns; that is `database/sql`'s behaviour, not the driver's. Scanning NULL
  into `sql.Null*`, pointers, `any` and plain types is pinned by the builder's
  `TestNulls`, which I read and found correct.

  **Answers to your points**
  1. **One row per `insert`:** confirmed. The pass refuses a second row ("second
  row"), and `stmt.Insert.Values` is a single `[]Term`. `insert.run` no longer
  does a `get` before writing; it writes through `insertNew` (`insert … on
  conflict do nothing returning 1`), and only the primary key can conflict
  there. A false result is UNIQUE, `ErrTooBig` is too big, and the count of
  changes on failure is 0, as in SQLite. A row cannot be stored when an error is
  reported: the check runs before the write, and a failure rolls back the
  transaction or the statement's savepoint. In a read-only transaction the sink
  is built and never run, so the unused `readOnly.insertNew` returning false
  does no harm.
  2. **Null:** `buildValues`, `eval(*Null)`, `checkType` (NULL passes every
  type) and `check` (NULL in the key or a `not null` column gives NOT NULL, in
  table order) are right. A nullable column of each type holding NULL next to
  values reads back correctly with `*` and by name, and survives the replay.
  4. **Order of errors:** every pair of faults above matched SQLite.
  5. **Planted bugs:** both still plant what their names and comments say.
     - `skipUnique` still upserts without a check of the key, so it replaces the
  existing row; that is the same fault as on main.
     - `checkOutside` still checks the key with a `get` in its own read
  transaction and then upserts in the write transaction through `b.skipUnique =
  … || b.checkOutside`. That leaves the gap between check and write that the bug
  is meant to plant.
     - It has one side effect, unchanged from main: a record that is too long
  with a key that exists gives UNIQUE where SQLite gives too big. It does not
  matter.
  6. **The tests the builder changed:**
     - `TypeRule` returns "" whenever a `not null` column or the key gets NULL
  or is left out. That sends the call to the comparison with SQLite; it does not
  excuse it.
     - `refusal(nil)` returning "" also sends the call to SQLite.
     - `Faults` is used only to count which cases the generator reaches.
     - In `verifyRows`, NULL is allowed only where the column takes it, and the
  key's `Nullable` is never set, so NULL in a key is reported.
     - `oracle.columns` treats the key as not nullable, whatever
  `pragma_table_info` says.
     - I found nothing that excuses what the rules do not allow.

  **Things that do not matter now**
  - **Low, latent:** `insert.check` checks every row before `run` writes any.
  When inserts of several rows come, SQLite checks and writes row by row. Row 1
  with a key that exists and row 2 with NULL in a `not null` column would then
  give UNIQUE in SQLite and NOT NULL here. Nothing reaches this today; it is for
  when several rows are built.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The generated checks of slice 6 are built and committed on branch
  `null-checks`, starting from `origin/null-engine` (39fee85) and ending at
  8a6dae5. Nothing is pushed. None of the checks found a difference from SQLite
  or mattn, or a bug in the engine, the driver or the core. `make test`, `make
  lint` and `make check-browser` pass, and so do the `-count=20` and `-race`
  runs.

  **What I built**
  - **`stmttest.Sequence`** (`internal/sql/stmt/stmttest/sequence.go`):
    - A table of more than one column has none, all, or about half of its
  columns outside the key taking NULL, a third of the tables each. One-column
  tables have none.
    - One create in ten turns whether one column takes NULL.
    - An insert gives NULL to a column that takes it 1 time in 4, by the literal
  or by a nil `?`. A list leaves such a column out 1 time in 3. One insert in
  12 has NULL for any column, the key too.
    - A call around a NULL argument gets a nil `[]byte` instead, which s4ql
  refuses. I took `nil` out of `Unsupported`, since it is now NULL.
    - `Generator.Create` now passes `NotNull` for each column outside the key.
  The pass's own pinned numbers did not move.
  - **`rule.go`**: `Faults` now names "NULL in the key" separately. The new
  `NullCases` gives the NULL cases an insert reaches, and both differential
  tests use it.
  - **Engine `TestDifferential`**: it counts the NULL cases by outcome, create
  tables by how many columns take NULL, and selects of `*` and of names that
  return NULL. Each has a least number of about a third of the run. A further
  4,000 sequences from seed 1,000,000 found nothing.
  - **drivercheck `TestGenerated`**:
    - `withNils` turns each nil argument into one of 10 kinds: nil, nil
  pointers, a nil `*sql.NullString`, and `sql.Null*` values that are not valid.
    - Each nil `[]byte` becomes one of three kinds: direct, `new([]byte)`, or a
  valid `sql.Null[[]byte]` holding nil.
    - Each row is scanned into `any`, then again into `sql.Null*` values and
  into pointers, also in `firstRow`. Both sides' scans are compared through
  `outcome.full`. A temporary mutant confirmed the comparison fails when the
  scans differ.
  - **Simulation**: `verifyRows` counts "NULL values in the log". The first
  1,000 seeds gave 1,581, 316 and 2,694 in the default, idle and hammer
  workloads. I require a quarter of that per seed: 0.4, 0.08 and 0.67.
  - **New planted bug `zeroLeftOut`** (`bugs.go`, `buildSink`, with a test in
  `TestBuild`): a column that takes NULL and is left out of a list gets the zero
  value of its type instead of NULL. It is planted only in standalone
  statements, like `listOrder`. It is found by a query that differs from SQLite
  and by the replay of the log; no check of non-NULL values can see it.
  - **`FuzzTypes` and `FuzzTypes_core`**: five new tables whose first column
  takes NULL, and a new form `formNull` (the literal, a nil argument, or the
  column left out). One minute each found nothing: 114,686 and 5,001 executions.

⏺ Agent "Mutation testing slices 5 and 6" is waiting on its own background work
