  (the `checkText` wrappers and `indexRefusal`), plus small ones in
  `docs/todo.md` and `internal/core/README.md`; `internal/core/tx.go`,
  `tx_test.go` and `index.go` merge cleanly. Once #92 is in `main`, merge `main`
  and take `main`'s version of the engine and driver files. `git diff
  origin/index-stmt...index-core` is my change alone.
  Local, pre-merge: `make test`, `make lint`, `make check-browser` pass; new
  tests `-count=50` and `-race -count=20` pass; asan in Docker for
  `internal/core` and `internal/format` (tests and fuzz) passes.

  **Commit format** (version stays 0; new `oneof` members, which an old reader
  refuses as newer):
  - `CreateIndex{index=1, table=2, columns=3}`, `IndexColumn{column=1 (position
  in the table's columns), descending=2 (absent when ascending)}`,
  `DropIndex{index=1}`.
  - `DropTable` drops its indexes and the commit does not list them: SQLite does
  it, and a list could only disagree with the cache.
  - Replay applies changes in their order. `Tx.Commit` writes: drops of indexes,
  then tables as before, then new indexes in creation order.
  - The reader refuses as malformed: no name, no table, the name of its own
  table, no columns, a column without a position, a column twice.
  - Replay refuses as `ErrLogBroken`: table or column missing, name of a table
  or index, reserved name, more than 250 columns, drop of a missing index,
  create table under an index's name.

  **Core API** (`internal/core/index.go`):
  - `Tx.CreateIndex(ctx, *s4qlpb.CreateIndex) error`, `Tx.DropIndex(ctx, name
  string) error`
  - `Indexes(ctx, table string) ([]*s4qlpb.CreateIndex, error)` and `Index(ctx,
  name string) (*s4qlpb.CreateIndex, error)` on `Tx` and `ReadTx`
  - `ScanIndex(ctx, table string, scan IndexScan) iter.Seq2[[]*s4qlpb.Value,
  error]` on both, with `IndexScan{Index string; Equal []*s4qlpb.Value; Lower,
  Upper Bound; Backward bool}`
  - `RowsRead() int64` on both; new `ErrIndexExists`, `ErrNoIndex`.

  **Order of creation:** not stored. It is the rowid order of the indexes in
  `sqlite_schema` (a new row gets max+1). `TestIndexes_order` covers drops, a
  reused rowid, close and replay; `cacheDump` reads it a second way, from
  `pragma_index_list` reversed.
  **Proof of index and bounds:** `ReadTx.ScanPlan` runs `explain query plan` on
  the core's statement on the core's connection. The plan must be exactly one
  row, e.g. `SEARCH main.t USING INDEX i (a=? AND b>? AND b<?)`, so no sort.
  52,988 generated scans (400 seeds, every pin count, bound shape and direction,
  five types, NULL, descending) match a plain SQLite database's rows in order.
  26 hand-made mutants were all killed.
  **Counts:** `RowsRead` is cumulative per transaction: one per row a scan
  stepped to, one per row `Get` found. SQLite's own counters are not compared
  (no `SQLITE_ENABLE_STMT_SCANSTATUS`, no `sqlite3_stmt_status` in the binding;
  binding untouched).

  **Decisions recorded** (`docs/todo.md`, "Decisions of the core step of
  indexes, slice 9"):
  - Columns are positions, as in `UpsertRows`.
  - An index dropped and created again, even identically, is a drop plus a
  create, because it moves to the end of its table's order.
  - Indexes are read and checked when their table is first looked up, not at
  `Open`; a foreign index then stops the instance with `ErrCacheVersion`.
  - At most 250 columns per index (`ErrInvalid`); SQLite takes 2,000.
  - `Tx.CreateTable` under an index's name gives `ErrIndexExists`.
  - `Tx.DropIndex` gives `ErrNoIndex` for anything that is not an index of the
  database, including a table and SQLite's primary-key index.
  - A NULL bound, and a NULL pin on a column that does not take NULL, are
  `ErrInvalid`; a row whose bounded column is NULL is within no bound.
  - Every scan (`Scan`, `ScanRange`, the commit's scan too) uses `indexed by`.
  `order by` holds only unpinned key columns; above 63 of them SQLite sorts
  (pinned by a test, correct rows).
  - `internal/core` had no fuzz target: I added `FuzzScanIndex` and put
  `internal/core` in `FUZZDIRS` in the Makefile, which is outside the listed
  directories.
  - Simulation: every workload now creates, drops and scans by indexes. Four
  reach minimums added; `deletes of the key -0.0` removed from `fullReaches` (0
  in the 30 default seeds now).

  **For the engine builder:**
  - `CreateIndex` errors come in SQLite's order: `ErrNoTable`,
  `ErrReservedName`, `ErrTableExists`, `ErrIndexExists`, `ErrInvalid`. `no such
  column` is the engine's; ask `Tx.Table` / `Tx.Index` first to keep the order.
  - Bounds are values: `Lower` is the smaller value even on a descending column.
  Pin NULL with a NULL in `Equal`; never send a NULL bound.
  - The key of an index is its columns plus the missing primary-key columns; an
  empty `Index` means the primary key.
  - `Indexes` order is the tie-break order; `Table` is unchanged; the 250-column
  limit and `ErrIndexExists` from `CreateTable` need mapping.
  - Planted bugs go in `scanConditions` (bound turned) and `table.scanKey` (no
  primary-key suffix). The simulation only scans whole indexes so far.

  **For the reviewer:**
  - The `Tx.spoolCommit` order with `spoolCreatedIndexes` / `forgetIndexes` /
  `DropIndex`: can any transaction's net effect fail at replay or give another
  index order? My own review's 4,000-seed random test found none.
  - The rowid-order claim in `conn.loadIndexes`.
  - The dropped `fullReaches` entry.
  - `loadIndexes` adds 1+n schema queries per first table lookup per transaction
  (in todo as not decided, not measured).
  - `FuzzScanIndex` ran only 319 inputs in 30 s under asan in Docker (150–400/s

⏺ The format and storage core of indexes are built (#97), and a Fable reviewer
  has started on it. Since you want to be in on data formats, here is the shape:

  - In a commit: CreateIndex with the index's name, its table, and its columns,
    each as a position in the table plus a descending flag; and DropIndex with
    the name. Dropping a table drops its indexes without listing them, as SQLite
    does.
  - Order of indexes: not stored. It is the order of the indexes in SQLite's own
    schema table, checked across drops, close, and replay. I asked the reviewer
    to challenge that claim in particular, because the engine's tie-break
    depends on it.
  - Scans: the core now names the index in every scan, also on the primary key,
    so SQLite never chooses. Tests read SQLite's query plan to prove it searched
    the index and bounds we asked for: about 53,000 generated scans match a
    plain SQLite database row for row.
  - Rows read is counted per transaction, ready for the pinned counts we agreed
    on.

  One limit the builder set: at most 250 columns per index, where SQLite takes

› Message from fabrik:builder
  I stopped after job 1: the fix is in PR #95 and all of its CI passed, but I
  did not merge it, so job 2 (#92) was not started.

  - **Cause:** SQLite writes a new table into its schema with an internal UPDATE
  whose text it formats under the length limit. That text is 96 bytes plus the
  table name twice plus the kept CREATE text, each in quotes with `'` doubled.
  The cache's own CREATE statement is longer than the user's, so under the fuzz
  target's limit of 1000 its UPDATE ran over and the core gave SQLite's raw code
  18 for a table SQLite accepts.
  - **Was it on `main`:** yes. Corpus file `188a29aaa6925c1d` (a generator seed)
  failed on `origin/main`, so the fix is on branch `fix-create-too-long` from
  `main`. The open todo item was a near miss: the cache statement here was only
  about 690 bytes; the UPDATE around it was what went over.
  - **What SQLite gives:** measured with the binding on Linux in Docker, SQLite
  refuses at prepare with `string or blob too big` exactly when that UPDATE is ≥
  limit. That covers a long table name (limit reached at 281 bytes), a long
  column name, and quotes in the name; the text with two 200-byte names gets
  `ok`. On macOS SQLite took texts up to about 24 bytes longer, because Apple's
  zone allocator rounds buffer sizes up.
  - **Before:** the core gave raw `sqlite: string or blob too big (code 18)` and
  failed the transaction, also for tables SQLite takes. The engine never gave
  the error itself.
  - **Now:** s4ql matches SQLite. The engine gives SQLite's `string or blob too
  big` exactly where SQLite does, after the column errors. The cache holds what
  SQLite takes; for a statement it couldn't read back under the limit it gives
  `core.ErrInvalid` and the transaction goes on. Replay creates tables the same
  way.
  - **Fix:** 85 added non-test lines, 44 of them code.
    - Pass: new `CreateTable.SQL` in `internal/sql/stmt` (stmt.go,
  recognize.go); the pass's tests compare it with `sqlite_schema`.
    - Engine: `schemaTooBig` in `internal/engine/errors.go`, called in
  operator.go; the plan carries `SQL`.
    - Core: `internal/core/cache.go` gets `changeSchema` (create and drop run
  under SQLite's largest limit) and declares a one-column key inline;
  `internal/core/tx.go`.
    - Binding: `internal/sqlite/options.h` sets `SQLITE_WITHOUT_ZONEMALLOC`, so
  macOS uses SQLite's own allocator as Linux does.
    - Tests: one subtest each in `TestConn_SetLengthLimit` and
  `TestEngine_tooBig` (86 texts against SQLite), two new subtests in
  `TestTx_tooBig` plus a rewrite of the old one, and the corpus file.
    - Todo: I removed the part of the open item about `Tx.CreateTable` giving
  code 18 and added a narrower open item. A table SQLite holds whose cache
  statement is longer than a lowered limit (many columns, many nullable) gets an
  internal error. The generated tests can't reach it; `FuzzSQL` could.
    - Diary: `docs/diary/2026-11-20-fix-create-too-long.md`, committed.
  - **Checks:** `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser` passed.
  Locally, `FuzzDifferential_core` ran 5 minutes (58,713 executions, no
  failure). In Docker on Linux, `go test -asan -short ./internal/sqlite
  ./internal/engine` and the engine's 10 fuzz targets under `-asan` at 30s each
  passed.

⏺ Agent "Fix fuzz finding, land #92" finished · 1h 12m 30s

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)
  ⎿  Resuming agent aab2596

⏺ The fuzz finding is fixed and merged (#95, a9b36a1, all 17 checks passed). It
  was on main already, not caused by the index work.

  - Cause: when SQLite stores a new table in its schema, it formats an internal
    statement that holds the table's name twice plus the create text, and that
    statement counts against the length limit. The cache's own create text is
    longer than the user's, so under the fuzz target's lowered limit of 1,000
    bytes the core failed with a raw SQLite error for a table that SQLite itself
    accepts.
  - Fix: s4ql now gives string or blob too big exactly where SQLite does, and
    the cache creates and drops tables under SQLite's largest limit.
  - One thing you should know about: the fix sets a build option of our SQLite
    (SQLITE_WITHOUT_ZONEMALLOC), so macOS uses the same allocator path as Linux.
    Without it, SQLite on macOS accepted texts about 24 bytes longer than on
    Linux, because Apple's allocator rounds buffers up. That is the same kind of
    platform difference the slice 8 reviewer found. It changes nothing on
    Linux, where s4ql runs in production.

  I merged it without a separate review, on green CI including the fuzz jobs and
  the builder's own review, to unblock the index chain; we can revert if the
  reviewer of the index core turns up a problem in that area. The builder is now
  landing #92.

  **Findings (all low)**

  1. `conn.load` / `conn.loadIndexes` (`internal/core/cache.go`,
  `internal/core/index.go`): every first look-up of a table in a transaction now

  `Get` took about 27-50 µs with `loadIndexes` switched off, 40-55 µs with it
  and no index, 57-72 µs with one index, 94-116 µs with five (5,000 iterations
  each, one machine, noisy). So a point read on a table with five indexes costs
  roughly 2 to 2.5 times what it did. `docs/todo.md` already lists this as "not
  decided, not measured"; these are the numbers. Fix: read the indexes only when
  a call needs them (`ScanIndex`, `Indexes`, DDL, `DropTable`).

  2. `RowsRead` and the doc of `ReadTx.ScanIndex` (`internal/core/index.go`):
  when more than 63 key columns are left unpinned, SQLite sorts, so it reads
  every row in the bounds before the first step. Confirmed by running: a loop
  that breaks at the first of 12 rows reports `RowsRead` 1, with the plan `SCAN
  ... USING COVERING INDEX wide_i` + `USE TEMP B-TREE FOR ORDER BY`. "The scan
  reads no more rows than are within them: RowsRead counts them" is not true
  there. Only matters for an engine test that pins the work of a `limit` over
  such a wide key. Fix: say so in the doc of `RowsRead`.

  3. `conn.loadIndexes`: a plain index that someone adds to the cache by hand
  (not unique, no collation) is taken as an index of the database. Dropping it
  through s4ql would then write a `DropIndex` that every other instance refuses
  with `ErrLogBroken`. Not run; read from the code. A hand-added table already
  behaves the same way, so this is not new exposure and I would leave it.

  **What I checked and found sound**

  - **Net effect and replay.** I wrote a random test with a model of its own: up
  to 12 transactions per seed of create/drop table, create/drop index, upsert,
  delete, savepoint, rollback to, release, and rollback, with tables and indexes
  competing for 7 names in two spellings. After each commit it compared the
  writer's view, the cache file (through `cacheDump`, which reads the order
  another way and runs the integrity check), a second cache that replays
  increments, a fresh cache that replays the whole log, and `modelOf` the log.
  2,500 seeds passed with a follower, and 1,200 with the two caches alternating
  as writer. No commit was refused, and schema, rows, and index order always
  matched. A planted bug (emptying `forgetIndexes`) was caught at seed 24. I
  also traced the name-sharing cases by hand (index name freed by its table's
  drop and then taken by a table, and the reverse) and found no order of
  `tx.order` that emits a create before the name is free.
  - **Order by rowid in `sqlite_schema`.** True in every case I could construct:
  drops, a reused rowid, dropped tables in between, close and open, replay. Run
  against the binding: `vacuum` renumbers the rowids (6, 8, 9, 10 became 3, 4,
  5, 6) and keeps their order; nothing in the code vacuums anyway. Storing the
  order would remove a dependence on SQLite's internals, but I see no real risk
  today, and `cacheDump` plus `TestIndexes_order` would catch a change.
  - **`ScanIndex`.** The plan text comes from the same SQL on the same
  connection, with no statistics tables, so `(a=? AND b>? AND b<?)` is what the
  statement seeks; open or closed is proven by the row comparison instead. I ran
  the plan for the 500-column key: all 500 pins are sought, and 499 pins plus a
  bound too; the sort appears only when more than 63 key columns are unpinned,
  and the seek stays. No input makes SQLite scan a whole index except a scan
  with no pins and no bounds, which asks for it. Quoting is safe: names are
  doubled-quote escaped, cannot hold NUL, the build has `SQLITE_DQS 0`, and the
  generated test uses names with quotes and a keyword.
  - **Refusals.** The format reader and replay refuse nothing a correct writer
  produces; both sides apply the same checks (250 columns, reserved names,
  shared names, columns in range, none twice).
  - **Foreign index.** Unique, partial, expression, collation, and constraint
  indexes stop the instance at the table's first look-up, which is before any
  write to it in that transaction or replay.
  - **Tests.** The generated test does compare with a separate in-memory SQLite
  and SQL of its own. `TestScanIndex_generated` takes 6.3 s plain, 33.5 s under
  `-race`, 1.7 s with `-short`; the whole package takes 145 s, so it does not
  hurt CI. `deletes of the key -0.0` stays in `singleReaches` and
  `hammerReaches`; it is a reach guard, and the property does not depend on the
  faults of the full workload, so no check that mattered is lost.

  Ready to merge once it is brought up to date with `main`: yes.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  - At this rate, internal/engine reaches the 10-minute timeout under asan soon.
  TestSimulation_plantedBugs runs 23 planted bugs, each searching up to 200
  seeds until 4 find the bug, so its time swings with the code.

  4. What the jobs repeat:
  - setup-go caches under a key of the Go version and the hash of go.sum. On
  main, that cache (42 MB) was saved once, at 11-19 06:54, by whichever job
  finished first. Every job since logs "Cache hit occurred on the primary key,
  not saving", so the heavy jobs start with almost no build cache. asan, test
  and fuzz each still download 20 modules, proto 86.
  - So SQLite's C code is compiled in about 11 jobs per run: test (its build
  step takes 37 s), asan, each of the 4 shards (about 78 s before the first
  fuzzing starts), fuzz (the 12 s heap check takes 46 s), sql, simulation,
  crosscheck, browser and site.
  - The changes job works: 49 of 193 runs (25%) skipped every code job, and site
  was skipped 26 times. 21 of the 69 code pushes to main tested a tree that a
  pull-request run had already tested.

  Proposed changes, ranked by what they buy (none removes or weakens a test):
  1. Split internal/core out of asan into a job of its own, or shard
  TestSimulation_plantedBugs' 23 bugs across 2 jobs with -run on the subtest
  names. Do the same for engine when it nears its timeout. Today core shares 4
  CPUs with about 560 s of other packages, so on its own it should get well
  below 591/600 s. The asan job should drop to roughly 6-7 minutes and stop
  deciding the wait in about 40% of runs. Risk: low, it only adds runners; the
  Makefile target must still run everything locally.
  2. Make the cache work: setup-go with `cache: false`, plus actions/cache keyed
  by job and flavour (-race, -asan, wasm) with restore-keys, saved on main.
  This saves about 0.5-1.3 minutes per heavy job, about 1 minute on the critical
  path, and about 10 runner-minutes per run. Risk: low, because Go's cache is
  content-addressed. The heap check `go test -run '^TestTx_Commit_large$'` is
  cacheable, so add `-count 1` to it, or a restored cache could reuse its pass.
  The other test commands use -shuffle, -count 1 or -fuzz, which go test does
  not cache.
  3. Site: lower sitecheck's -timeout from 10m to about 3m (about 3x the 63 s
  max) and dump the page's console and state and the Go goroutines on timeout.
  Each hang then costs about 3 minutes instead of 10.3 and leaves something to
  diagnose. It does not fix the hang. Also set `timeout-minutes` on every job,
  since today the default is 360. Risk: a slow but healthy check; none has
  passed 63 s.
  4. Fix the s3store flake (4 of 142 test jobs): make the test accept both
  orders, or have the client return ctx.Err() first. Which of the two is right

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The fuzz finding was a fault in the test, not in s4ql. Both PRs are open
  against `main` and neither is merged.

  1. `fix-fuzz-sequence`, PR #100 (CI: all 16 checks passed)
  - Cause: the test's rule `pair.typeRefusal` only checked the type of the key's
  value. So it expected no error for `'c'` going into the REAL column `t3.v3`,
  but s4ql refuses that under the exact-types rule, and SQLite 3.54.1 refuses it
  too ("cannot store TEXT value in REAL column t3.v3"). The sequence makes one
  table, drops nothing and rolls nothing back.
  - It no longer fails: 8514960 ("Check every value of an insert of several
  columns"), in #74 and merged right after #73, fixed it. The input fails at
  `ac45bbf` (the commit CI ran) and at `8514960^`, and passes at `8514960` and
  on today's `main`.
  - Fix: I added the input to `internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck/testdata/f
  uzz/FuzzSequence/c7060d373c508f7a`, rebuilt from the job log. Its SHA-256
  matches the name, so the bytes are exact. I also added one case to
  `TestTypeRule` (`internal/sql/stmt/stmttest/rule_test.go`): a text for a REAL
  column after a key of the right type.
  - What I ran: a local 5-minute fuzz run (9,184 executions, no failure). Then
  in the `golang:1.27.3` container: `go test -asan -short` for drivercheck
  passed in 159 s, and `make fuzz
  FUZZDIRS=internal/sqlite/crosscheck/drivercheck FUZZFLAGS=-asan FUZZTIME=30s`
  passed with 263 executions over 64 inputs, the new one among them.
  - Found and not fixed, now in `docs/todo.md`: a closed s4ql instance leaves
  its idle connections to the store open for 90 s, because each `Open` gets its
  own `http.Transport` and nothing closes it. During my fuzz run this added up
  to 32,604 open local connections. The machine ran out of ports, and other
  tests failed with `connect: can't assign requested address`.

  2. `fix-s3store-flake`, PR #102 (built on #100, which holds the diary; merge
  #100 first)
  - Cause: when the context ends, net/http still returns a response it has
  already parsed. The fake answers 200 with no headers as soon as the connection
  closes. So on a slow machine the client sometimes saw that answer after its
  deadline and reported "the answer has no ETag" without
  `context.DeadlineExceeded`. This hit `Get` in CI (run 44715954848), and
  `Create` had the same window.
  - Decision: the client was wrong. The core's per-request timeouts pass these
  errors on to its callers, where they look like a broken store. The core itself
  was not wrong: it decides by its own `ctx.Err()` and the `store.Store`
  sentinels.
  - Fix (`internal/store/s3store/s3store.go`, `content.go`): after the context
  ends, an answer that shows what happened is still used (a success,
  `ErrNotFound`, or a write refusal). Any other answer or error, and a read of
  the content that fails, now wraps the context's error through `ended` and is
  logged as `failed`/`unknown`, not `unexpected`. `unknown` no longer prints the
  context's error twice. The rule is in the package doc.
  - New subtest: it uses a test context whose `Err` reports `DeadlineExceeded`
  while its `Done` stays open. It deliberately breaks the `context.Context`
  contract, so check it in review. This forces the order every time, with no
  sleeps. Before the fix it failed with CI's exact error. The flaky subtest is
  unchanged.
  - What I ran: the two subtests with `-count=500`, then under load (20 busy
  loops on 10 cores, load average 18 to 41) `-count=500` and `-race -count=200`,
  all passing. The old client did not fail 500 times under that load either, so
  the forced subtest is what shows the bug.

  Self-review (code-review skill, medium) found six issues. I fixed three: the
  body read now follows the rule, the duplicated error text is gone, and a
  refused swap is checked. I left three edge cases that need the deadline to
  land in the microseconds between an answer and its check. They are listed in
  the diary.

  Both branches pass `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser`, #102
  after merging today's `origin/main` with #94. #100 was opened before #94
  landed and has not had `main` merged since, but its CI passed. The diary is
  `docs/diary/2026-11-20-fuzz-sequence-and-s3store-flake.md`: step 1 is on #100,
  step 2 on #102.

  The permission system refused nothing. The worktree guard rejected two git
  commands as too complex to verify. I split them into plain separate commands,
  which is what it asked for.

    S3 where it should report the ended context. The fix changes how the client
    reports errors after a context ends, and that sits right where "was my

    spec's rules for outcome unknown before it merges.

✻ Brewed for 4s · done Friday 5:21 PM

⏺ Agent "Land #97" is waiting on its own background work · 1h 2m 16s

› Message from general-purpose
  Review of PR #102 (`origin/fix-fuzz-sequence...origin/fix-s3store-flake`,
  s3store part). No finding that touches the correctness of the log. Worktree
  left clean, nothing committed.

  Findings, most severe first (none blocks the merge):

  1. Test design, low. `ending` in `/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/work
  trees/agent-a74aa1e4bd536107d/internal/store/s3store/s3store_test.go` breaks
  the `context.Context` contract, and a way that keeps it exists. Confirmed by
  running a throwaway internal test: a `Client` built by hand on
  `s3.Options.HTTPClient` with a fake `Do` that fires `WroteHeaders`, cancels a
  real `context.WithCancel`, and then returns a canned answer. It gave the same
  results as the subtest: no ETag and 500 wrap the context's error, 200 with
  ETag succeeds, 412 gives `ErrExists`/`ErrChanged`, 404 `NoSuchKey` gives
  `ErrNotFound`. It also runs the SDK under a context that is really done, which
  `ending` cannot, since anything that selects on `Done` does not see it. Fix:
  optional, swap `ending` for that fake HTTP client in an internal test. The
  subtest as written does prove its claim: with the client of the base branch
  put back it fails with `get "no-etag": the answer has no ETag` (confirmed by
  running).

  2. Simulation, low, no action needed. `simstore.Client.send` never hands back
  an answer once the context has ended: `sim.task.Wait` returns `waitErr(ctx)`
  even when the carrier is done. So "success or refusal after the request's
  context ended" is not modelled literally; only "context ended, outcome
  unknown, write may land late" is (`Faults.Late`). By reading, not by running:
  core cannot tell the difference, because `DB.send`, the take in `DB.acquire`,
  `Tx.upload` and `DB.releaseFrom` decide on the store's result alone and never
  look at `rctx` afterwards, and `Tx.publish` checks the caller's `ctx` only
  before it sends. The property is therefore checked for the equivalent state.

  What I traced and found sound:

  - Writes (`Client.put`): no classification changed. What was a sentinel, a
  success, or unknown before is the same now; only the wrapped context error and
  the log label differ. `unknown` still strips everything but the context
  error, so no error satisfies a store sentinel and a context error at once.
  Refusals and `ErrNotFound` are returned without the context error.
  - Core (`/Users/maragubot/Developer/s4ql/.claude/worktrees/agent-a74aa1e4bd536
  107d/internal/core/lease.go` `refusal`/`send`, `tx.go`
  `upload`/`publish`/`Commit`): decides by the store's sentinels and its own
  `ctx.Err()`, never by `errors.Is` on a context error from the store. The only
  such uses outside s3store are labels (`internal/engine/telemetry.go`,
  `internal/simulator/run.go`). A store error reaches the application only
  together with the end of the application's own context.
  - "An answer that shows what happened", confirmed by running with a real
  cancelled context: a 412 or 404 whose body breaks off becomes unknown/failed
  wrapping the context error; a 200 with ETag whose body breaks off on a put
  becomes unknown though applied (safe direction); a 404 with an empty body is
  not `ErrNotFound` (SDK code is `NotFound`). A 412 with an empty body is taken
  as a refusal, because the SDK derives `PreconditionFailed` from the status
  text; that predates this change and a 412 only answers a conditional request,
  so it is conclusive.
  - The three edge cases the builder left: none matters for the log. Each
  changes only which context error or label a non-sentinel error carries.
  - The originally flaky subtest: deterministic now, by reasoning. The fake is a
  TLS server and answers only after it sees the client's close, which follows
  the deadline (the window is between `close_notify` and the TCP close), so
  `ctx.Err()` is always set when the client checks, and `ended` adds the context
  error to every error path. Its 50 ms deadlines bound time only from the side
  a slower machine cannot break. Both subtests passed `-race -count=60` here,
  which supports this but does not prove it.

  Ready to merge as it is: yes.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  PR #101 (branch `ci-faster`) is open with reviewer markuswustenberg and is not
  merged. It is not ready to merge yet, for three reasons:
  - **TEMPORARY commit `5e3ab45`.** It makes this PR save its own caches so the
  second run can be measured. Revert it before merging.
  - **Measurement half done.** Run 2 (44774766445, warm cache, also holding the
  self-review fixes) was still running when I was told to hand back. Run 3, an
  empty commit, was never pushed. Brief item 7 is half done.
  - **Nightly never run.** I planned to validate it once with a temporary push
  trigger and did not get to it.

  Changes, one line each:
  - **`internal/testshard`.** `S4QL_TEST_SHARD=i/n` runs subtest k when k mod n
  = i-1. A malformed value fails the test, and so does a shard that holds no
  subtest. `TestSimulation_plantedBugs` in core and engine uses it (4 lines
  each).
  - **`make asan-test`.** New settings `ASANPKGS`, `ASANSKIPPKGS`, `ASANSHARD`,
  `ASANSHARDED`, `ASANTIMEOUT`. A stale package name fails, and so does a
  sharded run that runs no test. Shard 1 runs the package's other tests whole,
  without the variable. With no settings it runs exactly as before.
  - **`make fuzz`.** Shards are now assigned per fuzz test, round robin. New
  `make fuzz-shards` checks that `FUZZDIRS` holds every directory with a fuzz
  test (found with `git grep`), and that the n shards' selections add up to the
  unsharded selection exactly.
  - **Caches.** New actions `.github/actions/go` and `go-save` give each job and
  kind of build its own build and module cache, restored on PRs and saved on
  `main` only. Each entry is 40 to 340 MB, about 1.5 GB per set.
  - **`ci.yml`.**
    - `lint` and `test` are now inline, because a reusable workflow allows
  neither a timeout nor a cache. `test` passes the two AWS secrets as step env
  only and dropped the mattn build tags, which no file in the root module uses.
    - New jobs `asan-core` (3 shards) and `asan-engine` (2 shards). `asan` runs
  the other packages.
    - Every job except `browser` (untouched) has `timeout-minutes` of about 3x
  its measured maximum.
    - Every `go test` in CI has `-count=1`, `-shuffle` or `-fuzz`; the heap
  check got `-count=1`.
  - **`sitecheck`.** Timeout is now 3m (was 10m). On a timeout it prints its
  goroutine stacks, the requests the page made so far, and Chromium's output.
  - **`nightly.yml`.** Each fuzz test runs 5m (10x the PR's 30s; 6 asan shards
  plus one plain job). Planted bugs of core and engine run on 1000 seeds, plain
  build, sharded. A failed job opens or comments on an issue labelled `nightly`.
  New fuzz inputs are printed by `.github/scripts/fuzz-failures.sh`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/todo.md` now has a separate item for the `site` hang: not
  fixed, only cheaper and easier to diagnose. The `browser` item is kept.
  `internal/sqlite/README.md` describes the new CI jobs, and the diary has step
  1.

  How the shards are proven complete:
  - **Planted bugs:** the shard is chosen by index, not by a list of names. A
  test shows every index lands in exactly one shard for n up to 16. n comes from
  `strategy.job-total`, so a shard cannot go missing from the matrix.
  - **Packages:** `ASAN_CORE` and `ASAN_ENGINE` are workflow-level variables,

  `TestRows`, `TestOpen`, `TestOpen_cacheDir`, `TestTx`, `TestTx_wait`.
  Examples: `conn_test.go:316` expected `-1`, got `3`; `conn_test.go:1045`
  expected `1 a 1.5; 2 b 2.5; …`, got the reverse.
  - `TestTx_databaseSQL/should begin through a pinned connection…` hangs until
  the test timeout; the package ended with `panic: test timed out` in both runs
  (default 10 minutes, and `-timeout 150s`).
    - It is a hang of the test, not of s4ql: the order assertion at
  `tx_test.go:584` fails, `FailNow` runs the deferred `conn.Close()` of
  `tx_test.go:577`, and that blocks in `database/sql.(*closingMutex).Lock`
  because the `sql.Tx` on the connection is still open.
    - In CI this job would run into its timeout, not just fail.

  Fix: the helpers `values` and `rest` of the root tests read `select * from t1`
  unsorted, so sort there or compare as a multiset. Independently, end that
  test's transaction in a `defer` registered after the one for `conn.Close`, so
  an early failure cannot block the close.

  This supersedes "I confirmed only `TestReaders` there" in my report. The
  worktree is clean; I wrote only to my scratch directory.

✻ Worked for 2s · done Friday 5:48 PM

› Message from fabrik:builder
  I opened PR #104 (`s3store-close`) against `main` and did not merge it. Before
  that I landed #102: merging `origin/main` into `fix-s3store-flake` gave no
  conflict locally (GitHub's conflict was against an older `main`). I pushed
  that as 40d6c17, waited for all 16 checks to pass with `changes` at pass,
  merged it as 5e2f0b7 (state MERGED), and deleted the remote branches
  `fix-fuzz-sequence` and `fix-s3store-flake`.

  - **Who closes the transport:** the only place that makes one is
  `s3store.New`. The `Client` keeps that transport and closes it. `Client.Close`
  closes its idle connections at once, and closes every connection it dialed
  (kept in a map by its own `DialContext`) as soon as no request is in flight:
  at `Close` itself, or when the last request ends. A read counts as in flight
  until its body is closed. `Close` does not wait and refuses no call. A client
  built on someone else's HTTP client closes nothing of it.
  - **Wiring:** `Options.connector` hands the client to the connector, which
  closes it after `core.DB.Close`, also when that close failed. Connectors that
  tests build on their own stores close nothing.
  - **Review change:** my first version refused calls after `Close`. The review
  showed this breaks the core: `core.DB.Close` documents that a commit can keep
  checking its manifest swap after a close that timed out. Those calls would
  have failed, so a commit could retry forever or report a write that was never
  sent as unknown. I removed the refusal.
  - **Tests (no timing):** `TestClient_Close` checks whether each socket is
  closed without blocking. It covers two idle connections, `Close` during a held
  write (the write still succeeds and its connection closes after), and `Close`
  with a read open. Taking out the socket close failed the subtests: three of
  them with both close calls removed, and the open-read one in 6 of 20 runs with
  only the map close removed, so the map is needed. `TestOpen_s3` waits up to
  30 s for its server to see every connection closed after `db.Close`. A leak
  would hold them for 90 s, so a slow machine cannot break the bound. With the
  wiring removed it failed, but on only one of its two instances; the other's
  connection was already closed when the close cancelled its in-flight request.
  - **`ending`:** I removed it and the #102 subtest. `TestClient_endedContext`
  builds a `Client` on `s3.Options.HTTPClient` with a fake `Do` that fires
  `WroteHeaders`, cancels a real `context.WithCancel`, and returns a canned
  answer. It keeps every old case (now `context.Canceled`) plus the four you
  listed. Run against the client of before #102, it failed at its first case, as
  the old one did.
  - **Checks:** the new tests passed `-count=200` and `-race -count=100`, also
  under load, and `make test`, `make lint` and `make check-browser` passed. I
  deleted the todo item and wrote the diary at
  `docs/diary/2026-11-20-s3store-close.md`.
  - **Refused by the permission system:** two Bash commands with an inline
  Python here-doc and one that used `$(go list -m ...)` ("too complex to verify
  that it stays inside the worktree"). I ran the scripts from the scratchpad and
  split the other into plain commands.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

› Message from fabrik:builder
  Branch `index-engine`, PR #105 (https://github.com/maragudk/s4ql/pull/105),
  not merged; `origin/main` with #97, #94 merged in. `make test`, `make lint`,
  `make check-browser` pass; engine, simulator, root 3x and driver check 3x with
  the random draw pass; full `TestDifferential`/`TestSimulation` pass with pins
  unmoved; in Docker `go test -asan` of the engine and `make fuzz -asan` of the

  stopped (10 pass, 5 pending).
  1. Rules: `chooseAccess(t *s4qlpb.CreateTable, indexes []*s4qlpb.CreateIndex,
  conditions []condition) (access, error)` in `internal/engine/access.go`
  (parts: `never`, `pointOf`, `pinOf`, `indexKey`, `fit`, `tighter`); tests
  reach it as `engine.ChooseAccess`, `engine.AccessOf(src, ct, indexes, args)
  (Access, bool)`, `PathAccessOf`, `Rows.Access()`. `TestChooseAccess` has 56
  cases.
  2. Seed: `engine.Options.Directions *uint64` (exported option of the internal
  engine, nothing in the public package); without it one draw of `host.Uint64`
  per query, with it a splitmix64 sequence. Engine tests: every instance has a
  seed, logged on failure, flag `-directions`; the engine's simulation and the
  site simulator derive it from the run's seed. Forced path: `engine.Path{Full,
  Primary, Index, Limited, Pins, Forward, Backward}` via
  `Options.Path`/`Engine.SetPath` (`ScanAll`/`SetScanAll` kept as `Full`).
  3. Multiset: `answer.String` (engine) and `outcome.String`/`full` (driver
  check) hold the rows sorted, each row a quoted injective encoding, compared as
  one text: a wrong row changes an element, a missing or doubled row the length
  or an element. The driver check's old encoding was not injective (NULL = text
  "nil"); fixed. Root tests: sorted `query`/`values`/`rest`/`rowsText`,
  `inKeyOrder` where order is the point; engine hand tests that pin rows for
  another reason use a fixed forward path. `TestTx_databaseSQL` no longer hangs
  on a failed assertion (defer order), two engine test channels buffered.
  4. Pinned rows read (`TestEngine_rowsRead`; 4,000 rows): `k = ?` 1/1/1; `a =
  ?` 4,000 / 40 with (a) / 40; `a = ? and b >= ?` 4,000 / 40 / 22 with (a,b);
  key range of 100: 100/100/100. goqite receive without `order by` (3,000
  messages, 400 rows): 3,000 no index, 1,000 with goqite's index, 500 with an
  added `(queue, timeout)` (it pins as much and has a range, so the rules take
  it; slice 13 changes that).
  5. Schema reads, first statement of a transaction on a table with N indexes
  (`TestSchemaReads`): select by whole primary key 2 (as before slice 9); every
  other select 3+N; insert/update/delete 3+N (the foreign-index check runs
  before any row is written); later statements on the table 0.
  6. The coordinator's create-index-under-limit requirement: engine
  `indexTooBig` matches SQLite at every name length 400..480 in seven forms
  (macOS and Linux Docker); `conn.createIndex`/`dropIndex` through
  `changeSchema`; an index whose cache statement exceeds the limit is
  `ErrInvalid` before the log; restart and replay tested (core `TestTx_tooBig`,
  engine `TestEngine_indexes`); new `FuzzCreateIndex_core` with six seeds; the
  lander's todo item deleted.
  7. Decisions (todo, "Decisions of the engine step of indexes"): first
  condition in text order pins a column; "no row" cases are slice 7's only, `is
  null` of a NOT NULL non-key column pins nothing (keeps `stmttest.KeyAccess`
  valid); a whole-key `=` is a get even if an index pins more; one draw per
  query, update/delete forward; forced index on another table falls back to the
  rules; `index of more than 250 columns` at the 251st column after all SQLite
  errors (`core.MaxIndexColumns`); telemetry `statement.access`
  (none/primary_key/index/full_scan), `statement.index`, `statement.rows_read`

⏺ Agent "Indexes: engine and driver" finished · 1h 45m 29s

⏺ The engine and driver for indexes are built (#105, open, not merged). create 
  index and drop index run end to end, the engine picks its access by the agreed
  rules, and row order varies. It gets an independent Fable review after the
  reset; usage is at 99%.

  The first pinned performance numbers, as rows read from the cache on a table
  of 4,000 rows:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬────────┬─────────────────────┐
  │                  Query                  │  No    │    With an index    │
  │                                         │ index  │                     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ k = ? on the primary key                │ 1      │ 1                   │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ a = ?                                   │ 4,000  │ 40 with an index on │
  │                                         │        │  (a)                │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ a = ? and b >= ?                        │ 4,000  │ 22 with an index on │
  │                                         │        │  (a, b)             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────┤
  │ goqite's receive, without its order by  │ 3,000  │ 1,000 with goqite's │
  │ (3,000 messages)                        │        │  index              │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────┴────────┴─────────────────────┘

  The receive query is where the research's warning shows up in our own numbers:
  with an extra index on (queue, timeout) the rules take that one (500 rows),
  since it pins as much and has a range. That is right without order by, and it
  is the case slice 13's ordering rule exists for.

  Other points from the report:

  - Row order: a seed per engine makes any failure replayable. Rows are compared
    with SQLite as sorted text with an encoding that cannot make two different
    rows look alike. An old encoding in the driver check could: it wrote NULL
    and the text "nil" the same way. That is fixed.
  - The cost the reviewer found is gone: a select by the whole primary key reads
    the schema twice, as before indexes.
  - The create index length-limit fault is fixed the way the table one was,
    matching SQLite at every name length tried, on macOS and Linux.
  - One weakness the builder names itself: the key of an index is computed in
    two places, the engine and the core, and only tests tie them together. I'll
    ask the reviewer whether that should be one function.

⏺ Agent "Merge #106; triage #104, #105 failures" finished · 3m 16s

› Message from general-purpose
  Review of PR #104 (`s3store-close`, reviewed at c291491). The worktree is
  clean again. I committed nothing, pushed nothing and commented nowhere.

  1. **Medium: `internal/store/s3store/s3store.go`, `Client.leave`,
  `Client.Close` and `Client.shut`. Confirmed by running.**
     - **Scenario:** the client is closed and a request ends with no other
  request in flight. `leave` takes the map of connections, unlocks, and then
  closes the sockets directly. Those sockets are still in the transport's idle
  pool. A request that starts at that moment can take one of them from the pool
  and have it closed under it while it writes or reads.
     - **Reproduction:** a throwaway test, now deleted, closed the client and
  then ran 2 goroutines doing Get and 2 doing Create, 3000 calls each, against
  an httptest server. In 3 of 10 runs a Create failed with `outcome unknown: ...
  write tcp ...: use of closed network connection`, so a write whose outcome
  would have been known became unknown. The same load before `Close` gave 0
  errors.
     - **Where it matters:** only after a `core.DB.Close` that timed out, with
  two requests running at once. One example is a commit re-sending its swap
  while a reader's Get ends. The core treats the unknown as `sendUnknown` and
  sends the same swap again, and a failed Get in `observe` gives
  `observedNothing`. So the log stays correct. The cost is extra attempts, and
  the commit's caller may be told "unknown" if its context ends at that moment.
     - **Fix:** when the client is closed and nothing is in flight, call
  `transport.CloseIdleConnections()` instead of closing sockets directly. That
  takes connections out of the pool under the transport's own lock, so it never
  closes one that a request holds. I tried it and the stress test passed 15 of
  15 runs. The PR's subtest "should close the idle connections at once while the
  content of a read is open" then has to wait for the socket to close, because
  net/http closes an early-closed body's connection asynchronously.
  2. **Low: `TestOpen_s3`.** Waiting up to 30 seconds is on the safe side. A
  working close takes milliseconds even on a slow machine, and a leak lasts the
  90-second idle timeout. The test still only catches the missing wiring through
  the `Register` instance, as the builder notes. No path tests the cleanup when
  `newConnector` fails, or the close of the store after a failed `db.Close`. I
  read both and they are correct. Optional fix: a connector test with a fake
  `io.Closer` that checks it is closed once, after `db.Close`, also when that
  close fails.

  **Checked and found correct (read, and the tests run):**
  - **Counting requests in flight:** every `start` is matched by exactly one
  `end` on every return path of `Get` and `put`, and a read counts until
  `body.Close`. The SDK cannot retry inside a call (it uses `NopRetryer`), and
  redirects are not followed.
  - **Credentials:** the credentials providers share the same transport. A
  credentials fetch that runs on after its request ends can at worst fail and be
  fetched again.
  - **The map of connections:** it does not grow. The transport and `tls.Conn`
  close connections through the wrapper, which removes them. Under 4 concurrent
  writers the map held 4 connections.
  - **Dials after `Close`:** a connection dialed while a request is in flight is
  closed when the last request ends, and one dialed with nothing in flight is
  refused. No leak remains apart from a body that the core never closes, which
  is as before.
  - **Wiring in the connector:** the store is closed after `db.Close` on every
  path, and closing it twice is harmless. A store that someone else passes in is
  never closed. An HTTP client of someone else is never closed either, and the
  nil `conns` map of a hand-built client is safe.
  - **`TestClient_endedContext`:** it covers every case of the old subtest and
  adds the cut-off refusals, a cut-off applied write, and a 404 without the S3
  code. Each test call really runs under a context that has ended, so it proves
  what it claims. The one thing lost: the old test went through net/http itself,
  and this one bypasses the transport.
  - **Races:** `-race -count=200` of `TestClient_Close` and
  `TestClient_endedContext` passed, and so did `-race -count=30` of
  `TestOpen_s3`.

  Ready to merge once its checks pass: yes, with finding 1 as a small follow-up,
  since the core already tolerates the unknown it causes.

⏺ fabrik:builder(Fix review finding, land #104)
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Review s3store close" finished · 7m 18s

⏺ The review of the connection closing (#104) found one real fault, confirmed by
  running: after Close, the client closed its sockets directly while they were
  still in the HTTP transport's idle pool, so a request starting at that moment
  could get a socket that was then closed under it. In 3 of 10 stress runs a
  write came back as outcome unknown that would otherwise have been known. The
  core tolerates that and the log stays correct, but it is wrong, and it only
  shows after a close that timed out with requests still running.

  The reviewer also tried the fix: let the transport close its own idle
  connections, which never touches one a request holds. A builder applies it,
  adds the stress scenario as a test, and lands #104.

✻ Cooked for 22s · done Friday 9:15 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  Review of PR #105 (`origin/index-engine` at 2cf5533): nothing found that gives
  wrong rows, loses a row, or leaves a cache or log that cannot be read.
  Nothing committed, pushed, or commented; my throwaway tests are deleted and
  the worktree is clean.

  **Findings, most severe first (none blocks the merge)**

  1. **The engine's generated checks never read through an index** (process, not
  a bug). `stmttest.Sequence` leaves `Generator.Indexes` off, so
  `TestDifferential`, the fuzz targets, and the simulation create no index.
  Index reads in the engine are covered only by hand: `TestEngine_paths` (56
  `where`s), `TestEngine_indexes`, `TestEngine_rowsRead`. The model of the
  rules, the planted bugs in `fit`/`indexKey`, and the golden plans from the
  roadmap are not built either; `docs/todo.md` says so. Fix: do not mark slice 9
  built in the roadmap, README, or site until the follow-up builder lands.
  2. **`engine.indexKey` (`internal/engine/access.go`) and `core.table.scanKey`
  (`internal/core/index.go`) are the same dozen lines written twice.** If they
  diverged, `Equal` values would go to the wrong columns; core's `checkScan`
  catches that only when the column types differ, otherwise rows are lost
  silently. They agree today (confirmed by running, with the primary key inside
  an index, descending, and pins reaching the trailing key). The risk becomes
  real with primary keys of several columns. Fix: one exported
  `core.IndexKey(ct, ci)` used by both, before that slice.
  3. **`statement.rows_read` counts rows the cache gave, not index entries
  SQLite looked at** (`conn.scanIndex`, `TestEngine_rowsRead`). The pinned
  numbers (40, 22, 1000, 500) correctly show the engine's choice of index, pins,
  and bound. They would not move if SQLite stopped seeking and filtered a whole
  index, because the same conditions are in the cache's statement. Only core's
  `ScanPlan` check in `TestScanIndex_generated` guards the seek. Not run;
  follows from the code, and `RowsRead`'s comment says it. Fix: assert
  `ScanPlan` for the pinned engine queries in the follow-up.
  4. **No seed for driver tests: matters little.** Root and `drivercheck` tests
  compare multisets or accept both orders, and a direction-dependent failure
  recurs about every second run. Root `-short -count=6 -shuffle` and
  `drivercheck -short` 8 times all passed. Smallest honest remedy: add the drawn
  direction to the statement event beside `statement.access`; no public seed.

  **What I checked, by area**

  - **Bounds cannot lose rows, by construction.** Every term handed to
  `ScanIndex` is one of the `where`'s own conditions (the first `=` or `is null`
  on the column, or one condition's bound). SQLite evaluates it in `select …
  indexed by … where … order by …`, and the filter re-checks every condition. A
  wrong pick in `tighter` could only be looser; direction and `desc` are
  SQLite's `order by`, not hand-made seeks.
  - **Random test of my own, passed.** 8,000 `where`s (two seeds), 1 to 4
  conditions on 6 columns with repeated columns, contradictions, `null` literals
  and arguments, -0.0/0.0, empty text and blob, and int64 extremes. 200 rows,
  11 indexes (ascending, descending, key inside, three columns). Each ran by the
  rules, full scan, primary key, and (each index path on a random third of the
  queries) every index with every pin limit, forward and backward. Rows matched
  SQLite as a multiset and, on forced paths, in SQLite's `order by` of the
  path's key; 1,668 of the second seed's 4,000 gave rows.
  - **Values across types.** Infinite, NaN, NUL-in-text, and out-of-range values
  are refused as "not supported" identically with and without indexes; no
  internal error (run).
  - **Multiset encodings are injective.** In `answer.String`, null is bare, text
  is Go-quoted, blobs are `x'hex'`, reals are `real <shortest digits>`, and
  each row is quoted. In `rowKeys`, `rowText`, and `outcome.text`, each value
  carries its type and is quoted inside a bracketed row. Sorting cannot hide a
  wrong, missing, or doubled row.
  - **Order where it is specified is still compared exactly.** Core's
  `TestScanIndex_generated`, `TestEngine_paths`, `TestEngine_order`, and
  `instance.state` against `oracle.state` all compare in order. `pin` no longer
  checks key order for its hand-written rows; the paths test covers that.
  - **Direction.** One draw per query in `Engine.reading(true)` (`query`,
  `queryIn`), used by the plain scan and every access. `update`, `delete`, and
  `checkOutside` use `reading(false)`, and `changeRows` changes rows in key
  order whatever the access. Only tests and `internal/simulator` set
  `Directions`; root `conn.go` passes only `Logger`.
  - **Rows left open through an index** read all 1,000 rows, forward and
  backward, while another connection ran `drop index`, `drop table`, `create
  index`, `update`, or `delete` (run).
  - **Lazy index loading (read, plus scenarios run).** Every upsert, insert,
  delete, scan, and index read takes its table from `conn.lookUp`, which loads
  the indexes, and `checkIndexed` guards the rest. Tables are forgotten in
  `changeSchema`, `RollbackTo`, and at the end of a transaction. `Get`, `Table`,
  and `Tables` skip the foreign-index check, but they neither write nor read
  through an index.
  - **Transactions and replay (run).** 14 transactions mixing drop and create of
  indexes and tables with reused names matched SQLite's rows and indexes after
  a restart on the same cache and after replay into an empty one. A statement
  that fails after `create index`, a rollback of `create index` and of `drop
  index`, and the tie-break after drop and create plus replay all gave the same
  access.
  - **Statements.** Every difference from SQLite I found is a "not supported"
  refusal: `main.i`, a column named twice, reserved names, `nulls first`, an
  expression.
  - **`indexTooBig` and `changeSchema`.** Read only; `LengthLimit` is not a
  public option. The one gap is the open item already in `docs/todo.md`
  (internal error where the cache's quoted statement exceeds the limit).
  - **Existing suites.** `go test -short -race ./internal/engine
  ./internal/core` passed, and the engine's `-short` tests passed with
  `-directions=1` to `5`.

  Ready to merge once its CI passes: yes.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  `changes` at pass; `main` already had the new jobs. CI passed on the first
  run.
  - **What closes connections now:** `Client.leave` calls
  `transport.CloseIdleConnections()` when the client is closed and no request is
  in flight, and `Close` calls it too. The map of dialed connections, the
  client's `DialContext`, the type `conn` and `errClosed` are gone
  (`internal/store/s3store/s3store.go`). The diary's claim that the map was
  needed for the open read does not hold with the new close. net/http closes
  that connection itself in its own goroutine, so the subtest waits for it, with
  a limit of 30 s against the 90 s idle timeout. Its check of a dial that ends
  after the close now goes through the real transport, with a held dial in a
  test dialer. That case relies on net/http's undocumented `closeIdle`
  behaviour; the subtest pins it.
  - **Stress test** (1 goroutine doing Gets and 1 doing Creates, 8,000 each,
  after `Close`): on the old client it failed in 40 of 40 runs without `-race`
  and 40 of 40 with it. After the fix, 0 of 20 both ways. A run takes about 1.2
  s, or 5 to 6.6 s under `-race`. Smaller counts were not reliable (13 of 20 at
  4,000 each).
  - **Package tests:** `-count=200` and `-race -count=100` pass. The `-race` run
  takes about 11 minutes, so it needs `-timeout 40m`; my first try ended at the
  default 10-minute timeout with no test failing. One extra subtest fix came
  out of the 200 runs: a read that was read to its end failed once in 200 runs,
  because its connection was not yet closed when the content's close returned.
  It now waits for it too.
  - **Connector test (optional item):** done. The close-on-failure logic moved
  into `ownedConnector` in `conn.go`, which the tests reach through
  `OpenOwnedStore` in `export_test.go`. New subtests of `TestClose` check the
  store is closed once, after `db.Close`, also when the close fails, and when
  the connector cannot be made.
  - **Self-review:** code-review at low found that `leave` might run before
  net/http returns the connection to the pool. Covered: a read's content calls
  `leave` only after closing the body, and the transport closes any connection
  that becomes idle after `CloseIdleConnections`. I wrote that into the `Close`
  doc comment and changed nothing else.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  The statement pass of slice 10 is done on branch `if-exists-stmt`, PR #107
  (reviewer markuswustenberg, not merged). `make test`, `make lint` (0 issues),
  `make check-browser` and `make fuzz-shards` (c/4, go/2) pass, and so does asan
  in the `golang` image for `internal/sql/stmt` and `stmttest`.

  - **Fields:** `CreateTable.IfNotExists`, `CreateIndex.IfNotExists`,
  `DropTable.IfExists`, `DropIndex.IfExists`, all bool. They have no span, since
  no other clause of the typed form has one. The old reject names `IF NOT
  EXISTS` and `IF EXISTS` are gone from the pass.
  - **Schema text:** SQLite stores the text from the name on, without the
  clause. `create table If Not Exists t (...)` is kept as `CREATE TABLE t
  (...)`. So `SQL` needed no change, and `checkCreateTable` and
  `checkCreateIndex` compare it with `sqlite_schema` for every supported text.
  - **Decisions (in `docs/todo.md`):**
    - A bare `if` as the name after IF NOT EXISTS is refused as `bare name IF
  after IF NOT EXISTS`. SQLite parses it, then fails on its own stored text with
  `malformed database schema (if) - near "(": syntax error` and creates
  nothing.
    - `drop table if exists if` and `drop index if exists if` are supported.
    - Everything slices 1 to 9 refuse is still refused after the clause, with
  the same name and offset (TEMP, schema, AS SELECT, UNIQUE, and so on).
    - Known difference: a declaration s4ql does not support stays "not
  supported" even when the object exists and SQLite would do nothing. Examples:
  an unknown type, a second primary key, not STRICT, COLLATE, a partial index,
  `(v, v)`.
  - **Point 2, all measured and pinned:** these are syntax errors that the pass
  gives exactly as SQLite does:
    - the clause in the wrong place: `create if not exists table`, `drop table t
  if exists`
    - half a clause: `if exists` on create, `if not exists` on drop, `if not`,
  `if`
    - the clause twice
    - a name `not` or `exists` after the clause
    - `create table if (` with no clause.
    - TEMP and `main.t` stay not supported.
  - **Point 3, the table for the engine builder** (pinned in a subtest of
  `TestCheck`):

  | Case | What SQLite does |
  |---|---|
  | `create table if not exists`, table exists | Nothing, no error. No duplicate
  column, too many columns, more than one primary key, or unknown type error. |
  | `create table if not exists`, no table, an index has the name | `there is
  already an index named i` (SQLite checks for the table first, then for an
  index of the name) |
  | `create table if not exists`, any database | `object name reserved for
  internal use` and `unknown table option` still come |
  | `create index if not exists`, index exists | Nothing, also with a missing
  column |
  | `create index if not exists`, other cases | Still errors: `no such table:
  main.x`, `there is already a table named t`, `unsupported use of NULLS FIRST`;
  `no such column` when the index does not exist |
  | `drop table if exists` on an index name | Nothing |
  | `drop index if exists` on a table name | Nothing |
  | Drops of reserved names | Still errors: `table sqlite_master may not be
  dropped`, `index associated with UNIQUE or PRIMARY KEY constraint cannot be
  dropped` |
  | `changes()` | Never changed, whether the statement does something or nothing
  |
  | `pragma query_only`, statement would do nothing | Succeeds |
  | `pragma query_only`, statement would write | `attempt to write a readonly
  database`, after the name and column errors |

  - **Engine:** `existsRefusal` in `internal/engine/exists.go` is called once at
  the end of `check`, and `TestCheck_exists` pins it. It gives the old `IF NOT
  EXISTS` / `IF EXISTS` error at the IF.
    - Outside `internal/sql/stmt`: the driver's `check` in `conn.go` calls
  `engine.Check` again, as in slice 8, because drivercheck pins the error at
  prepare.
    - Outside `internal/sql/stmt`: the slice-8 `checkText` wrappers are back in
  the engine's differential test and in `drivercheck_test.go`, because
  `stmttest.Others` holds two texts with the clauses.
    - The engine's pinned numbers did not move.
  - **Generator unchanged with the option off:** with `Generator.Exists` at 0, a
  dump of 64,440 lines from six settings plus `Sequence` over 30 seeds is
  byte-identical before and after my change. It is also identical to the output
  of `origin/main`'s code once the new `false` fields are stripped.
    - In `stmttest/sequence.go` I changed only the comment on `Others`.
  - **Pins and review:** the pass's own pinned numbers moved (mutants 39→29 and
  153→151, near misses 1531→1760); the diary has the breakdown.
    - The code-review skill found four nits, all fixed.
    - The README writer caught a wrong error order in my doc comment, also
  fixed.
  - **Permissions:** some compound Bash commands (heredocs with backticks, piped
  `git show`) were refused as too complex to verify. I split them up or used
  script files; I did not work around a refusal.

⏺ Agent "Slice 10 statement pass" finished · 40m 9s

⏺ The statement pass of slice 10 is done (#107): s4ql recognises if not exists
  and if exists on tables and indexes, checked against SQLite's parser. A small
  agent is merging it, and I told the index checks builder what it changes in
  the files they share.

  Two things from it:

  - A known difference from SQLite, recorded in the todo. When the table already
    exists, SQLite does nothing and doesn't even look at the declaration. s4ql
    sees only the text, so a declaration it doesn't support (say, a table that
    isn't strict) is still refused, where SQLite would have stayed silent. For
    glue this means its migrations table must be declared in a form s4ql
    supports, which it must anyway to be created the first time.
  - An oddity in SQLite itself. create table if not exists if (...), a table
    named if, parses but then fails on SQLite's own stored text with "malformed
    database schema". s4ql refuses that text up front.

  The engine step of slice 10 waits for the index checks to land. Meanwhile I'll
  start the statement pass of slice 11, default, once #107 is in, since it only
  touches the statement package.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  No real fault found: no difference from SQLite on any path, in any order, or
  in any cache plan, and the engine's choice never differed from the model.

  Branch `index-checks` is pushed as PR #108
  (https://github.com/maragudk/s4ql/pull/108), with `markuswustenberg` as
  reviewer, not merged. It includes `origin/index-engine` (twice) and
  `origin/main` up to #107. `make test`, `make lint`, `make check-browser` and
  `make fuzz-shards` (c in 4 and 6 shards, go in 2) pass after the last merge.
  `make asan-test` for engine and core, and fuzz shards 3/4 and 4/4 under asan,
  passed in Docker.

  - **Every path:** `pair.everyPath` runs each generated WHERE on the second
  engine before the call. That covers the WHERE of a select, and the WHERE of an
  update or delete as its own select, since both find rows by the same scan. It
  runs by the rules and by a sample of `candidates`: every row, the primary
  key, and each index with each number of pins, forward and backward,
  deduplicated. Results must match SQLite's rows as a multiset, and a forced
  path must match SQLite's `ORDER BY` of its key. The call itself then runs on
  the other engine by the next of `otherPaths(index names)`.
    - Sampling: 4 random candidates from the pair's own seed when there are more
  than 4, otherwise all. The fuzz targets run all of them (`pair.allPaths`).
    - TestDifferential: 1,138 WHERE, 443 of them on indexed tables; 349 went
  through the sample.
  - **Model:** `stmttest.ChosenAccess` (with `Access.Same`) is written from the
  roadmap text and the todo decisions. `checkChoice` compares it with
  `engine.AccessOf` on SQLite's schema for every WHERE: index, pinned values and
  bounds.
  - **Plan of the cache:** `core.ReadTx.ScanPlan` moved into `index.go`. One
  candidate in four, plus the rules' access one time in four (outside
  transactions, on the core), must give exactly the `wantPlan` row, and so must
  every pinned query of `TestEngine_rowsRead`.
  - **Planted bugs:**
    - `pinByPlace` (engine `fit`, a pin taken from the wrong column): found by 7
  of 60 sequences in `TestDifferential_plantedBugs`, each rerun clean. Found by
  19 of 200 engine-simulation seeds, all by its own violation.
    - `turnDescBound` (core `scanConditions`): 33 of 200 core-simulation seeds.
    - `shortKey` (core `indexKey`, which `core.IndexKey` calls without bugs): 29
  of 200 core-simulation seeds.
    - None found anything without its bug. All are in the README tables. The
  core simulation now scans with pins and bounds against the model's own filter.
  - **Golden plans:** `TestAccessOf_golden` covers 7 schemas and 62 plans in
  `internal/engine/testdata/plans`, goqite's receive included. Update with `go
  test ./internal/engine -run TestAccessOf_golden -update`, as the README says.
  - **Tallies:** least numbers (a third, or 1) in TestDifferential and
  TestConn_differential cover:
    - each create/drop index error, and read-only refusals;
    - each kind of choice and each kind of path: 0–3 pins, ranges, a range on a
  descending column, NULL pins, backward;
    - plans, replays and restarts with indexes, and commits and rollbacks with
  indexes.
    - The simulation counts an index dropped while rows are open (83/14/122 per
  1,000 seeds) and integrity checks every seed.
  - **Fuzz:** `FuzzIndexes` and `FuzzIndexes_core`, 13 seeds, checked by
  `TestFuzzIndexes_seeds`. They are in shards 3/4 and 4/4 (5/6 and 6/6), and
  `TestEngineLayers` lists them.
    - `FuzzIndexes` ran 37,679 execs in 1 minute.
    - `FuzzIndexes_core` ran 2,044 execs in 1 minute, or 14,544 in 3 minutes
  with `-fuzzminimizetime 1x`.
  - **Time:** the machine was at load 13–25, so these are noisy.

› Message from fabrik:builder
  I built the statement pass of slice 11 on branch `default-stmt` and opened PR
  #109 against main, with markuswustenberg as reviewer. It is not merged.
  `origin/main` (including #108) is merged in. `make test`, `make lint` (0
  issues), `make check-browser`, both `make fuzz-shards` runs and `make
  asan-test` for stmt and stmttest all passed. After the merge I reran short
  tests of the sql packages, engine, core, root and crosscheck, and lint, but
  not the full `make test`.

  **The field:** `stmt.Column.Default` has the new type `stmt.Default{Value
  stmt.Value; Span stmt.Span}`. `Value` is nil when no DEFAULT is written and
  `stmt.Null{}` for DEFAULT NULL. `Span` covers the literal with its sign, and
  that text is exactly SQLite's `dflt_value`. The literal is read by insert's
  own code, split out of `recognizer.literal` into `sign` and `value`.

  **Decisions (in `docs/todo.md`, slice 11 group):**
  - Every form insert refuses keeps its name after DEFAULT: `+ sign`, `NULL
  after a sign`, `hex literal`, digit separator, outside 64 bits, infinite real,
  string or blob after a sign, `TRUE or FALSE`, `current date or time`.
  - New names:
    - `name after DEFAULT`: a bare, double-quoted, bracketed or backticked word,
  which SQLite stores as text.
    - `DEFAULT of an expression`: `(1)`, `(1+1)`, `(null)`, `(?)`.
    - `second DEFAULT`: SQLite keeps the last one.
    - `DEFAULT of another type than its column`: by the exact-types rule. SQLite
  accepts every pair at create and converts or refuses at insert; the
  measurement gave no reason against refusing.
  - DEFAULT NULL is supported on a NOT NULL column and on the key, as SQLite
  accepts it at create; leaving the column out gives NOT NULL failed.
  - A DEFAULT on the key is supported. DEFAULT may come anywhere among the
  constraints, once.
  - `insert ... default values` stays `DEFAULT VALUES`.

  **What SQLite did with the point-2 cases (pinned shell):**
  - Accepted at create: everything else, including `-1`, `+1`, `- 1`, -2^63,
  `-0.0`, `true`/`false`, hex, every real form, `'it''s'`, blobs, `"abc"`, a
  bare word, `(1)`, `(1+1)`, `current_timestamp`, DEFAULT twice, any order of
  constraints, and NULL on NOT NULL or the key.
  - Syntax errors: `default ?`, `default` alone, `- -1`, `+-1`, `~1`, `not
  null`, `-?`, `-(1)`, `()`, `'x' 'y'`, `1.5e`, `x'0'`.
  - `default (?)` gives "default value of column [v] is not constant".
  - Under a length limit of 1,000, a create text over the limit gives "string or
  blob too big".

  **For the engine and core builders (all in the todo):**
  - An insert that leaves a column out stores its default of each kind. `-0.0`
  reads back as 0.0, as an insert of `-0.0` does.
  - An explicit NULL is not replaced by the default.
  - DEFAULT NULL on a NOT NULL column or the key gives NOT NULL failed, naming
  the first such column in table order.
  - A key default gives UNIQUE failed on the second insert.
  - Order of errors: the text and arguments, no such table, no column named,
  number of values; then readonly before every row error; NOT NULL before the
  record being too big. A record can become too big because of a default
  (500-byte default plus a 600-byte value).
  - An insert without a column list, an update and a select are unaffected.
  - `dflt_value` is the literal as written, from sign to end: `-1`, `+1`, `- 1`,
  `- /* c */ 1`, `1.0`, `.5`, `1E+3`, `-0.0`, `00012`, `'it''s'`, `x'00'`,
  `X'aBcD'`, `true`, `TRUE`, `null`/`NULL`/`Null`. `(1)` gives `1`, and `default
  1 default 2` gives `2`.
  - The cache tells a nullable column by a `null` default, but a `not null
  default null` column has that text too. The core will need another way to tell
  the two apart.

  **Engine:** `defaultRefusal` in `internal/engine/defaults.go`, pinned by
  `TestCheck_defaults`, gives `DEFAULT` at the keyword as before. The code
  review found that I first called it after `existsRefusal`, which turned
  goqite's IF NOT EXISTS plus DEFAULT shape into an IF NOT EXISTS error. It now
  runs first, giving `DEFAULT (at offset 48)` as on main.

  **Generator unchanged with the option off:** with `Generator.Defaults` at 0, a
  58,920-line dump (8 settings plus `Sequence`, 30 seeds) is byte-identical to
  the old one after stripping the new zero field from the `%#v` output. The
  pass's tests set it to 3. That moved the pinned excused counts: mutants 29→41
  and 151→154 (same kinds of message as before), near misses 1,760→2,130 (the
  new `default (?)` form adds 272, and there are more create tables). The README
  explains each.

  **Outside `internal/sql/stmt`:** I renamed three equivalent mutants in
  `internal/sql/mutation/equivalent.txt` from `recognizer.literal` to
  `recognizer.value`. Without that, `TestEquivalent` failed with "no such
  mutant".

  **Permissions:** shell commands that wrote files with heredocs were refused as
  too complex to verify; I wrote those files with the Write tool instead.
  Nothing else was refused.

⏺ The statement pass of slice 11 is done (#109): a column declaration may carry

  Decisions of the pass that are stricter than SQLite, all recorded in the todo:

  - A default of another type than its column is refused at create table. SQLite
    takes any pair and converts or refuses at insert. Under our rule of exact
    types such a default could never be stored, so refusing it early is the
    consistent choice.

    refused as not supported, like the same forms in insert.

    column out of an insert then fails with the NOT NULL error.

  the default text null, and that can't stay, since a column can now be not null
  default null. The core builder has to pick a clean way for the cache to

› Message from fabrik:builder
  No difference from SQLite was found that I could not resolve. Every generated
  check found no difference from SQLite: 300 differential sequences, 60 in
  transactions, 250 in the driver check, and 1,000 simulation seeds per
  workload.

  Branch `if-exists-engine`, PR #110
  (https://github.com/maragudk/s4ql/pull/110), reviewer markuswustenberg, not
  merged. `make test`, `make lint`, `make check-browser` and `make fuzz-shards`
  (c/4, go/2) pass after the last change. `make asan-test
  ASANPKGS=maragu.dev/s4ql/internal/engine` in golang:1.27.3 passed (308 s)
  before the review fix, and that fix is Go only.

  **How nothing reaches the log:** `buildSink` returns the sink `nothing`. On
  its own, `Conn.nothingToWrite` builds the sink in a read transaction first and
  succeeds there without taking the writer, as SQLite only reads the schema. If
  the reader is busy or the sink is not `nothing`, the write transaction looks
  again and decides, and rolls back without an annotation. In a write
  transaction it releases its savepoint unannotated. In a read-only one it
  succeeds. The core writes no commit object for a transaction without changes.

  **Error order per form:**
  - create table: the pass's text errors, then the table there (nothing), then
  `there is already an index named`, then column errors, then too big.
  - create index: `no such table`, then `there is already a table named`, then
  the index there (nothing, also with a missing column), then `no such column`,
  then too big, then more than 250 columns.
  - drop table / drop index: nothing where no table / no index has the name,
  also where the other kind has it.
  - In read-only transactions, `attempt to write a readonly database` comes
  after all of these.

  **Decisions, recorded in `docs/todo.md`:**
  - The sink sits at SQLite's place in its order of errors.
  - A statement of its own reads first and waits for no writer; the write
  transaction decides. The review found the first version waited for the writer.
  - In a write transaction it releases its savepoint unannotated; in a read-only
  one it succeeds.
  - `ErrRowsOpen` still applies in a transaction, because the rule is checked
  before anything is read.
  - No `string or blob too big` for a statement that does nothing; 251 columns
  does nothing where the index exists.
  - `RowsAffected` and `LastInsertId` behave as for the plain forms.
  - `statement.nothing` is true, false, or absent.
  - `ExistsRule` reads SQLite's schema before each statement, which is how I
  read "track". Check this is what you meant.
  - `replaceExisting` was chosen over "writes to the log", because the latter
  shows only in a transaction with other writes. The simulation's check for it
  caught a hand-planted case at seed 0.
  - `FuzzIndexes` was extended using bits 4, 8 and 16; the old seeds read as
  before.
  - The driver's `engine.Check` and the `checkText` wrappers are back for slice
  11's `defaultRefusal`, which landed meanwhile.

  **Planted bug:** `replaceExisting` is found by 123 of 200 default seeds, 87 by
  "the replay of the log holds", and by 0 without the bug.

  **Tallies:** every did-its-work, did-nothing-with-its-reason and error kind of
